# 06.Persistence — State Map

> **What this file is:** Phase and task tracker for all work within `06.Persistence`.
> **What it is not:** The root tracker — that lives at `state-map.md`.
> **Sync policy:** When all tasks under a Phase Key are `●`, run `/state-map-phase` with `phase_key: SK.06.{Phase}` to propagate that milestone to the root state-map.

---

## Legend

| Symbol | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `○` | Not started |
| `◐` | In progress |
| `●` | Complete |
| `⚑` | Blocked |
| `—` | N/A / Skipped |

## Phase: Design <!-- phase-key: SK.06.Design -->

> Finalize all interface shapes, interceptor contracts, specification evaluator behavior, and DI extension signatures before any implementation begins.

| ID | Task | Work Order | Package(s) | State |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-01 | Design `StronglyTypedIdValueConverter<TId, TValue>` — implicit-operator-based EF value converter with no reflection; companion `StronglyTypedIdValueConverterSelector` for `ModelConfigurationBuilder` auto-registration | WO-008 (P-033) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-02 | Design `ValueObjectOwnershipConvention` — `IEntityTypeConfiguration` model-building convention that auto-applies `.OwnsOne()` for `IValueObject` properties; nested value object handling; document `OwnsMany` limitation | WO-008 (P-033) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-03 | Design `AuditSaveChangesInterceptor` contract — `ISaveChangesInterceptor` populating `IHasCreatedAudit` on Added entries and `IHasAudit` on Modified entries; `IUserContext` scoped DI injection pattern; `IClock` injection; `FallbackUserIdentifier` fallback strategy | WO-008 (P-033) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-04 | Design soft-delete query filter convention — auto-applies `e => !e.IsDeleted` global query filter for `ISoftDeletable` entities; `IgnoreSoftDeleteFilter()` extension on `IQueryable<T>` for admin/audit paths | WO-008 (P-033) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-05 | Design concurrency token convention — auto-applies `.IsRowVersion()` (SQL Server) or `.UseXminAsConcurrencyToken()` (PostgreSQL/Npgsql) based on provider; unknown provider falls back to `.IsConcurrencyToken()` with startup warning — **SUPERSEDED by D-65 (WO-051/P-315)**: a provider-branching convention living in `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` is architecturally impossible — `.UseXminAsConcurrencyToken()` is an `Npgsql.EntityFrameworkCore.PostgreSQL` extension method, and `EfCore` must never reference `Npgsql`. The never-built `ConcurrencyTokenConvention` this task (and `EntityTypeConfigurationBase`'s own XML doc) assumed is retired outright; see D-65 for the corrected split (EfCore: provider-neutral `.IsConcurrencyToken()` marking only; PostgreSQL: new `XminConcurrencyTokenConvention`/`XminRowVersionValueConverter`) | WO-008 (P-033) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-06 | Design tenant query filter convention — `AddTenantIsolationFilter()` opt-in extension; global query filter `e => e.TenantId == currentTenantId` for `IHasTenant` entities; `ITenantProvider` sourced from DI; not applied by default | WO-008 (P-033) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-07 | Design `IRepository<TAggregate, TId>` and `IReadRepository<TAggregate, TId>` interface contracts — write-side vs read-side separation; no `IQueryable` exposure; specification-driven read surface; generic constraints `where TAggregate : IAggregateRoot<TId> where TId : notnull` | WO-013 (P-066) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions` | `●` |
| D-08 | Design `IUnitOfWork`, `IDbConnectionFactory`, and `ISpecificationEvaluator<T>` interface contracts — single-method save boundary; open-connection factory for Dapper; evaluator contract for alternative implementations (e.g., Cosmos DB) | WO-013 (P-066) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions` | `●` |
| D-09 | Design `SharedKernelDbContext` abstract base — three-interceptor constructor registration (Audit, SoftDelete, Concurrency; no OutboxInterceptor); `ApplyConfigurationsFromAssembly` in `OnModelCreating`; `SaveChangesAsync` override semantics | WO-013 (P-067) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-10 | Design `EntityTypeConfigurationBase<TEntity, TId>` — auto-apply primary key, concurrency token, soft-delete filter, audit columns, tenant column; `base.Configure(builder)` call contract for downstream configurations | WO-013 (P-067) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-11 | Design `EfRepository<TAggregate, TId>` and `EfReadRepository<TAggregate, TId>` abstract base classes — write-only vs read-only surface; `ISpecificationEvaluator<T>` composition; `AsNoTracking` application | WO-013 (P-069) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-12 | Design `SpecificationEvaluator<T>` evaluation order — strict pipeline: Criteria → Includes → OrderBy/ThenBys → Distinct → AsNoTracking → Skip/Take; paging-last invariant | WO-013 (P-069) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-13 | Design `TenantedDbContext` and `ICurrentTenantService` — runtime-captured (not startup-snapshot) global query filter; `TenantedRepository<TAggregate, TId>` with `GetByIdForTenantAsync` bypass method | WO-013 (P-070) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-14 | Design `EfCorePersistenceBuilder` fluent DI API — `AddSharedKernelEfCore<TContext>` entry point; `.WithMultiTenancy()` opt-in; `.Build()` registration shape; startup guard for TContext/TenantedDbContext mismatch; no-op `IUserContext` placeholder strategy | WO-013 (P-073) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-15 | Design audit string adapter contract (P-091) — resolve `IUserContext.UserId` type change from `string` to `Guid` (Security.Abstractions) without altering `string` audit columns; format rule: `userId.ToString("D")` when `IsAuthenticated == true && UserId != Guid.Empty`; fallback `"system"` when unauthenticated or empty; `NoOpUserContext` returns `UserId = Guid.Empty`, `IsAuthenticated = false`; audit column `HasMaxLength(256)` confirmed sufficient for 36-char GUID string | WO-016 (P-091) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-16 | Design `Guid.Empty` no-tenant sentinel contract (P-092) — document behavioral decision: after P-078 migrates from `ICurrentTenantService` (nullable `Guid?`) to `ITenantProvider` (non-nullable `Guid`), the no-tenant state is `Guid.Empty`; global filter becomes `e.TenantId == Guid.Empty` when provider not configured, returning zero rows; no rows should ever have `TenantId == Guid.Empty` in production; `NoOpTenantProvider` returns `Guid.Empty` explicitly | WO-016 (P-092) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-17 | Design `ExistsAsync` and `GetByIdsAsync` repository interface extensions (P-093) — `IRepository<TAggregate, TId>` gains `ExistsAsync(TId id, CancellationToken ct) → Task<bool>`; `IReadRepository<TAggregate, TId>` gains `GetByIdsAsync(IEnumerable<TId> ids, CancellationToken ct) → Task<IReadOnlyList<TAggregate>>`; EF Core: `ExistsAsync` uses `AnyAsync` (no materialization); `GetByIdsAsync` uses `Where(e => ids.Contains(e.Id)).ToListAsync` (IN clause, result order not guaranteed, missing IDs produce no entry, warn >1000 IDs) | WO-016 (P-093) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions`, `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-18 | Design `EfRepository.UpdateAsync` tracking optimization (P-094) — current unconditional `.Update(aggregate)` marks all columns Modified even for tracked entities; fix: check `DbContext.Entry(aggregate).State != EntityState.Detached` before calling `.Update()`; tracked entities rely on EF change detection; detached entities: call `.Update()` as before; expose as `protected virtual MarkAsModifiedIfDetached(TAggregate aggregate)` helper; no interface change | WO-016 (P-094) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-19 | Design `IProjectionSpecification<TAggregate, TResult>` interface (P-079) — lives in `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions/Specifications/`; extends `ISpecification<TAggregate>`; adds `Expression<Func<TAggregate, TResult>> Selector { get; }`; AOT-safe expression tree; prerequisite for projection-read methods on `IReadRepository`; also document IDbConnectionFactory doc-only fix: remove "exclusively by Dapper" restriction from XML doc | WO-014 (P-079) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions` | `●` |
| D-20 | Design bulk write interface additions (P-080, Cap 1) — add `AddRangeAsync(IEnumerable<TAggregate>, CancellationToken) → Task`, `UpdateRangeAsync(IEnumerable<TAggregate>, CancellationToken) → Task`, `DeleteRangeAsync(IEnumerable<TAggregate>, CancellationToken) → Task` to `IRepository<TAggregate, TId>` in Abstractions; same staging semantics as single-entity methods (mutations staged until `IUnitOfWork.SaveChangesAsync` called); no ORM dependency introduced | WO-014 (P-080) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions` | `●` |
| D-21 | Design projection read method additions to `IReadRepository` and `EfReadRepository` (P-080, Cap 2) — `ListProjectedAsync<TResult>(IProjectionSpecification<TAggregate, TResult>, CancellationToken) → Task<IReadOnlyList<TResult>>`; `GetBySpecProjectedAsync<TResult>(IProjectionSpecification<TAggregate, TResult>, CancellationToken) → Task<TResult?>`; depends on `IProjectionSpecification<TAggregate, TResult>` from P-079; `SpecificationEvaluator<T>` updated to handle projection: applies `.Select(spec.Selector)` after all criteria/ordering/paging steps; selector-after-paging rule is consistent with paging-last invariant (Skip/Take remain last in the `IQueryable` pipeline before Select is applied) | WO-014 (P-080) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions`, `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-22 | Design `ListPagedAsync` and `PagedList<T>` dependency strategy (P-080, Cap 3) — `ListPagedAsync(ISpecification<TAggregate>, CancellationToken) → Task<PagedList<TAggregate>>` on `IReadRepository`; `PagedList<T>` lives in `04.Contracts` (`SharedKernel.Contracts`); adding this to the interface in Abstractions requires adding `SharedKernel.Contracts` as a project reference to `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions.csproj`; this is within the root layering rules (06.Persistence may reference 01–05) but widens the Abstractions dependency surface — record as deliberate design decision; two round-trips: count query (spec without Skip/Take) + data query (spec with Skip/Take) under same DbContext scope | WO-014 (P-080) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions`, `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-23 | Design `IDomainEventDispatcher` hook in `EfUnitOfWork` (P-080, Cap 4) — after `_dbContext.SaveChangesAsync(ct)` succeeds, collect domain events from `ChangeTracker.Entries<IHasDomainEvents>()`; call `IDomainEventDispatcher.DispatchAsync(events, ct)`; clear event collection on each aggregate; dispatcher is optional (constructor injection via `IEnumerable<IDomainEventDispatcher>` or nullable single instance — document preferred injection strategy); `IDomainEventDispatcher` lives in `SharedKernel.Domain` (03.Domain) which EfCore already references; no `05.Application` reference introduced; no-dispatcher path unchanged | WO-014 (P-080) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-24 | Design `QueryableExtensions.IgnoreSoftDeleteFilter` removal and `IncludeDeleted` evaluator integration (P-080, Cap 5) — delete `QueryableExtensions` class entirely (IQueryable leak elimination); `ISpecification<T>.IncludeDeleted` property was added in P-095 (dispatched to `03.Domain/SharedKernel.Domain`); update `SpecificationEvaluator<T>`: when `spec.IncludeDeleted == true`, call `.IgnoreQueryFilters()` on the query before applying criteria; IgnoreQueryFilters position in pipeline: applied before Criteria step to avoid filter interference; document removal as breaking change | WO-014 (P-080) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-25 | Design `GetByIdAsync` removal from `IReadRepository` and `ByIdSpecification<TAggregate, TId>` convenience spec (P-080, Cap 6) — remove `GetByIdAsync(TId id, CancellationToken ct)` from `IReadRepository<TAggregate, TId>` (breaking change); add `ByIdSpecification<TAggregate, TId>` in `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions/Specifications/`; spec uses `Criteria = e => e.Id.Equals(id)` (expression tree, AOT-safe); callers replace `repo.GetByIdAsync(id, ct)` with `repo.GetBySpecAsync(new ByIdSpecification<TAggregate, TId>(id), ct)`; migration guide required in CLAUDE.md | WO-014 (P-080) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions` | `●` |
| D-26 | Design `GetProjectedQuery` promotion to `ISpecificationEvaluator<T>` contract (P-097) — add `GetProjectedQuery<TResult>(IQueryable<T> inputQuery, IProjectionSpecification<T,TResult> spec) → IQueryable<TResult>` to the `ISpecificationEvaluator<T>` interface in Abstractions; method already exists on `SpecificationEvaluator<T>` — promotion closes the downcast gap; zero ORM dependencies introduced (both parameters are BCL expression types or already-Abstractions types); alternative evaluator implementations (Cosmos, in-memory, Marten) must implement this method | WO-017 (P-097) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions` | `●` |
| D-27 | Design `EfReadRepository` field type change from concrete to interface (P-097) — the `_evaluator` field changes from `SpecificationEvaluator<TAggregate>` to `ISpecificationEvaluator<TAggregate>`; constructor parameter type changes accordingly; `ListProjectedAsync` and `GetBySpecProjectedAsync` call `_evaluator.GetProjectedQuery(...)` on the interface directly — no downcast; behavioral contract is identical; any `ISpecificationEvaluator<T>` implementation will now work without `InvalidCastException` | WO-017 (P-097) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-28 | Design `EfUnitOfWork` single-constructor collapse (P-098) — collapse the two-constructor form `(SharedKernelDbContext)` and `(SharedKernelDbContext, IDomainEventDispatcher?)` into exactly one constructor `(SharedKernelDbContext dbContext, IDomainEventDispatcher? dispatcher = null)`; nullable optional parameter is idiomatic .NET DI optional-dependency pattern; DI resolves `null` when `IDomainEventDispatcher` is not registered; resolves the concrete implementation when registered; eliminates the ambiguity where the container silently selects the shorter constructor and skips the dispatcher | WO-017 (P-098) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-29 | Design `ExistsAsync` predicate correction in `EfRepository` (P-099, Part 1) — replace `EF.Property<TId>(e, "Id")!.Equals(id)` (shadow property accessor, fragile on concrete CLR properties) with expression-tree `Expression.Property(param, "Id")` + `Expression.Equal(idProperty, Expression.Constant(id, typeof(TId)))` compiled to `Expression<Func<TAggregate, bool>>`; same pattern as `ByIdSpecification<TAggregate, TId>`; confirmed AOT-safe on EF Core `IQueryable`; behavioral contract unchanged | WO-017 (P-099) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-30 | Design `IPersistenceTransaction` and `ITransactionalUnitOfWork` interfaces (P-099, Part 2) — `IPersistenceTransaction : IAsyncDisposable` with `CommitAsync(CancellationToken ct) → Task` and `RollbackAsync(CancellationToken ct) → Task`; lives in `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions` using BCL types only (zero ORM dependencies); `ITransactionalUnitOfWork : IUnitOfWork` with `BeginTransactionAsync(CancellationToken ct) → Task<IPersistenceTransaction>`; `EfPersistenceTransaction` sealed class in EfCore wraps `IDbContextTransaction`; `EfTransactionalUnitOfWork` sealed class in EfCore implements `ITransactionalUnitOfWork`; domain event dispatch fires post-CommitAsync consistent with `EfUnitOfWork` semantics; `EfCorePersistenceBuilder.WithTransactionalUnitOfWork()` fluent method registers `ITransactionalUnitOfWork → EfTransactionalUnitOfWork` (scoped) | WO-017 (P-099) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions`, `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-31 | Design `TenantedRepository` soft-delete bypass semantics (P-100) — `GetByIdForTenantAsync(TId id, Guid tenantId, ct)`: bypasses tenant filter only; preserves soft-delete filter for `ISoftDeletable` entities via `IgnoreQueryFilters()` + re-application of `Where(e => !EF.Property<bool>(e, nameof(ISoftDeletable.IsDeleted)))` clause (EF Core cannot selectively bypass one filter — workaround is explicit re-apply); `GetByIdForTenantIncludingDeletedAsync(TId id, Guid tenantId, ct)`: bypasses both filters via `IgnoreQueryFilters()` — reserved for audit/recovery only; XML doc on each method must clearly state filter semantics; non-`ISoftDeletable` entities: both methods behave identically | WO-017 (P-100) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-32 | Design `ListPagedProjectedAsync<TResult>` addition to `IReadRepository` and `EfReadRepository` (P-101) — `IReadRepository<TAggregate, TId>` in Abstractions gains `ListPagedProjectedAsync<TResult>(IProjectionSpecification<TAggregate,TResult> spec, CancellationToken ct) → Task<PagedList<TResult>>`; `EfReadRepository` implementation: count query uses `GetQuery` (no projection, no Skip/Take) then `CountAsync`; data query uses `GetProjectedQuery` (with projection, with Skip/Take) then `ToListAsync`; both under same `DbContext` scope; `PagedList<TResult>.Create` called with projected items, page, pageSize, totalCount; depends on D-26 (P-097) — `GetProjectedQuery` must be on the interface | WO-017 (P-101) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions`, `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-33 | Design `ValueObjectOwnershipConvention` rename to `ValueObjectOwnershipBuilder` (P-102) — class renamed from `ValueObjectOwnershipConvention` to `ValueObjectOwnershipBuilder`; it does NOT implement `IModelFinalizingConvention` and does NOT auto-apply; XML doc updated to explicitly state: "static utility method, not an EF Core `IModelFinalizingConvention`; call `ValueObjectOwnershipBuilder.Apply(modelBuilder)` manually at the end of `OnModelCreating`; do not register via `ConfigureConventions` — it has no effect there"; CLAUDE.md references updated; all test references updated | WO-017 (P-102) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-34 | Design double-dispatch fix for `EfTransactionalUnitOfWork` and `EfPersistenceTransaction` (P-105 Fix 1) — `EfTransactionalUnitOfWork.SaveChangesAsync` must NOT call `DispatchAndClearEventsAsync` when `DbContext.Database.CurrentTransaction` is non-null (an explicit transaction is active); it dispatches only when `CurrentTransaction == null` (no open transaction); `EfPersistenceTransaction.CommitAsync` calls `DispatchAndClearEventsAsync` after the database commit succeeds; this is the standard EF Core transactional dispatch pattern; three test scenarios: begin→save→commit dispatches once; save without open transaction dispatches once; begin→save→rollback dispatches never | WO-018 (P-105) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |

New interface `IBulkMutationRepository<TAggregate, TId>` lives in `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` (NOT Abstractions — `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Query.UpdateSettersBuilder<TAggregate>` is an EF Core type and cannot appear in an Abstractions signature without violating the zero-ORM-dependency rule); `EfRepository<TAggregate, TId>` implements this interface directly as additional surface beyond `IRepository<TAggregate, TId>`. Abstractions interface gains `StreamAsync(ISpecification<TAggregate> spec, CancellationToken ct) → IAsyncEnumerable<TAggregate>` and `StreamProjectedAsync<TResult>(IProjectionSpecification<TAggregate, TResult> spec, CancellationToken ct) → IAsyncEnumerable<TResult>` — both are pure BCL-typed declarations (`IAsyncEnumerable<T>` is a BCL interface), zero ORM dependency. `EfReadRepository` implementation: `StreamAsync` calls `_evaluator.GetQuery(...)` then `.AsAsyncEnumerable()`; `StreamProjectedAsync<TResult>` calls `_evaluator.GetProjectedQuery(...)` then `.AsAsyncEnumerable()` — both backed by EF Core's native server-side streaming, no client-side buffering. **Forced AsNoTracking deviation (documented explicitly)**: both streaming methods call `.AsNoTracking()` on the resulting `IQueryable` unconditionally, regardless of `spec.AsNoTracking` — this is the one documented exception to "spec's AsNoTracking flag is honored"; rationale: streamed entities are enumerated incrementally and the `ChangeTracker` would otherwise accumulate an unbounded number of tracked entities for the lifetime of the enumeration. **Skip/Take + streaming semantics (decision: honored as row-window-before-streaming)**: `Skip`/`Take` (evaluator step 7) are applied as normal before streaming begins — the result is a bounded "page" of rows streamed incrementally rather than buffered; no special-casing or fail-fast needed since the evaluator pipeline already applies Skip/Take before any terminal operator. | D-58 | Design `IDataSeeder<TContext>` contract and `EfCorePersistenceBuilder` opt-in migration/seed startup orchestration (P-151) — CONFIRMED implementable as specified (verified `Database.MigrateAsync` compiles; `IHostedService`/`IDbContextFactory`/`IDbConnectionFactory` already established patterns). New interface `IDataSeeder<TContext> where TContext : DbContext` in `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore/Seeding/` with single method `SeedAsync(TContext context, CancellationToken ct) → Task`; XML doc mandates (as a documentation requirement, not enforced by the framework) that implementations must be idempotent — re-running `SeedAsync` against already-seeded data must be a no-op. `EfCorePersistenceBuilder<TContext>` gains two opt-in fluent methods: `.WithMigrationsOnStartup()` (sets a flag so `Build()` registers a hosted service that calls `Database.MigrateAsync()` on startup) and `.AddSeeder<TSeeder>() where TSeeder : class, IDataSeeder<TContext>` (registers `TSeeder` as scoped and appends its `Type` to an ordered list); when either method is called, `Build()` registers a single hosted service `MigrationAndSeedHostedService<TContext>` that, in `StartAsync`: (1) if migrations-on-startup was requested, calls `Database.MigrateAsync(ct)`; (2) runs each registered seeder's `SeedAsync` in registration order, each inside its own DI scope. **Multi-replica race mitigation (documented, no hard `02.Caching` ref)**: the hosted service acquires a PostgreSQL advisory lock (`SELECT pg_advisory_lock(hashtext(@lockKey))` / `pg_advisory_unlock`) via `IDbConnectionFactory` before running migrations+seeders and releases it after — this is the recommended default for K8s multi-replica deployments and requires no new package reference (`IDbConnectionFactory` is already in Abstractions, implemented by `NpgsqlConnectionFactory` in `.PostgreSQL`); CLAUDE.md documents an alternative optional integration point — consuming services may instead inject `02.Caching.Redis.DistributedLocking`'s `IDistributedLockService` and wrap the hosted service's logic themselves — but `06.Persistence` takes no reference to `02.Caching`. **Explicit non-goals** (documented in CLAUDE.md): not a migration-authoring tool (no `dotnet ef migrations add` wrapper); not a replacement for `WithCompiledModel` (P-106) — when a compiled model is used, `Database.MigrateAsync()` still applies pending SQL migrations, the compiled model only affects runtime model-building. **Fully opt-in**: omitting both `.WithMigrationsOnStartup()` and `.AddSeeder<T>()` leaves `Build()` behavior completely unchanged — no hosted service registered, no automatic migrations | WO-024 (P-151) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-59 | Design delegation of `EncryptedValueConverter`'s AES-256-GCM mechanism to `SharedKernel.Cryptography.ISymmetricEncryptionService` (P-227) — `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` takes a direct `ProjectReference` to `01.Core/SharedKernel.Cryptography` (legal: 06 may reference 01–05, no layering exception needed). `EncryptedValueConverter`'s constructor gains `ISymmetricEncryptionService` as a required dependency (resolved from DI, registered by `AddSharedKernelCryptography()` in the consuming service's composition root — `06.Persistence` does NOT call `AddSharedKernelCryptography()` itself; `.WithEncryption()` documents this as a prerequisite). The on-disk wire format `"v{version}:{Base64(nonce||ciphertext||tag)}"` is preserved exactly: the converter still owns wire-format packing/parsing (version-prefix string handling, Base64 encode/decode, byte-slice concatenation) — only the cryptographic primitive (nonce generation, AES-GCM encrypt/decrypt, tag verification, key lookup) moves to `ISymmetricEncryptionService`. Encrypt path: converter calls `symmetricEncryptionService.Encrypt(plaintextBytes)` → receives `EncryptedPayload { KeyId, Nonce, Ciphertext, Tag }` → converter packs `"v{KeyId}:{Base64(Nonce||Ciphertext||Tag)}"` itself (KeyId is treated as the version string — `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider` below uses `EncryptionOptions` version keys, e.g. `"v1"`, as `CryptographicKey.Id`/`KeyId`, so no translation layer is needed). Decrypt path: converter parses the version prefix and the packed `nonce||ciphertext||tag` byte blob from the stored string, reconstructs an `EncryptedPayload { KeyId = parsedVersion, Nonce, Ciphertext, Tag }`, and calls `symmetricEncryptionService.Decrypt(payload)` → `Result<byte[]>`; on `Result.IsFailure` (tamper, wrong key, unknown `KeyId`) the converter throws `EncryptionKeyNotFoundException` (preserving the existing exception contract — see D-61) rather than propagating a raw `Result` type through `ValueConverter<string,string>`'s synchronous, non-`Result`-aware API. Constructor: `(IOptionsMonitor<EncryptionOptions> optionsMonitor, IEncryptionVersionOverride versionOverride)`. `GetCurrentKey() → CryptographicKey`: resolves the target version as `versionOverride.OverrideVersion ?? optionsMonitor.CurrentValue.CurrentVersion` (the exact same precedence rule the old `EncryptedValueConverter.Encrypt` used directly — see D-62), looks up `optionsMonitor.CurrentValue.Keys[version]`, Base64-decodes it, and returns `new CryptographicKey(version, decodedKeyBytes)`. This is the mechanism by which the existing rotation-scoped `IEncryptionVersionOverride` seam (P-147) continues to direct which key a batch encrypts with — the override now flows through `IEncryptionKeyProvider.GetCurrentKey()` instead of being read directly inside the old hand-rolled `Encrypt` method. `GetKey(string keyId) → CryptographicKey?`: looks up `optionsMonitor.CurrentValue.Keys[keyId]` directly (ignores `versionOverride` — decryption always targets the exact `KeyId` recorded in the stored ciphertext's version prefix, never the current/override version); returns `null` (not a thrown exception) when `keyId` is absent from `Keys`, per `IEncryptionKeyProvider`'s documented contract — `EncryptedValueConverter`'s decrypt path treats a `null` return (surfaced via `ISymmetricEncryptionService.Decrypt`'s `Result<byte[]>` failure) as the trigger for `EncryptionKeyNotFoundException`. `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider` is registered scoped (matching `IEncryptionVersionOverride`'s existing scoped lifetime) by `EfCorePersistenceBuilder.WithEncryption()` — registered as `SharedKernel.Cryptography.IEncryptionKeyProvider`, distinct from any `IEncryptionKeyProvider` a consuming service might separately register for its own general-purpose `ISymmetricEncryptionService` usage outside the persistence layer (documented overlap risk — see Hard Violations). Hot-reload behavior (`IOptionsMonitor<EncryptionOptions>`) is preserved unchanged: every `GetCurrentKey()`/`GetKey()` call reads `optionsMonitor.CurrentValue`, never a captured snapshot. | WO-037 (P-227) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |

Old behavior: a direct dictionary-miss (`KeyNotFoundException`/manual lookup) inside the hand-rolled `Decrypt` static method. New behavior: `EncryptedValueConverter`'s decrypt path first calls `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider.GetKey(parsedVersion)` (via the `ISymmetricEncryptionService.Decrypt(payload)` call internally resolving the key) — when the version prefix parsed from stored ciphertext does not exist in `EncryptionOptions.Keys`, `ISymmetricEncryptionService.Decrypt` returns `Result.Failure(Error.Unexpected(...))` (per its documented contract — `IEncryptionKeyProvider.GetKey` returning `null` is one of the documented failure causes alongside tamper/wrong-key). `EncryptedValueConverter` inspects the failure and distinguishes "unknown key version" from "tamper/auth-tag mismatch" by calling `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider.GetKey(parsedVersion)` itself first (a synchronous, cheap dictionary lookup) before delegating to `ISymmetricEncryptionService.Decrypt` — if the key is absent, throw `EncryptionKeyNotFoundException(parsedVersion)` immediately without invoking `Decrypt` at all; only when the key exists does the converter delegate to `ISymmetricEncryptionService.Decrypt` and treat a `Result.Failure` there as a tamper/corruption signal (rethrown as `EncryptionKeyNotFoundException`-adjacent or a new tamper-specific exception is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE for this phase — acceptance criteria require only that the unknown-version case continues to throw `EncryptionKeyNotFoundException` exactly as before; tamper-detection exception shape is unchanged from pre-existing behavior and not redesigned here). | WO-037 (P-227) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |

| D-76 | Design the explicit-transaction retry-safety correction — `ITransactionalUnitOfWork.ExecuteInTransactionAsync` + `EfTransactionalUnitOfWork.BeginTransactionAsync` guard (P-320) — EF Core's retrying execution strategies require the ENTIRE transactional unit (transaction start through commit) to run inside one `IExecutionStrategy.ExecuteAsync(...)` delegate; the existing `BeginTransactionAsync()` → caller-held `IPersistenceTransaction` handle → `CommitAsync()` shape hands control back to arbitrary caller code between begin and commit, which is fundamentally incompatible with that contract — EF Core's own native `BeginTransactionAsync` already throws `InvalidOperationException` when called under a retrying strategy, but with EF Core's own vocabulary, not this platform's. New `ITransactionalUnitOfWork` member(s) — `Task<TResult> ExecuteInTransactionAsync<TResult>(Func<CancellationToken,Task<TResult>> operation, CancellationToken ct)` plus a non-generic `Task ExecuteInTransactionAsync(Func<CancellationToken,Task> operation, CancellationToken ct)` convenience overload (both BCL-only: `Func<>`/`Task`/`CancellationToken`, zero ORM types, lives in Abstractions) — the retry-safe alternative: `EfTransactionalUnitOfWork`'s implementation wraps `DbContext.Database.CreateExecutionStrategy().ExecuteAsync(async () => { <begin, invoke operation, commit — reusing the EXISTING begin/commit machinery refactored into a shared internal core method, including the P-105 deferred-domain-event-dispatch-until-commit behavior> })` — the whole delegate (including a fresh `BeginTransactionAsync`) re-runs on each retry attempt, which is exactly what the execution-strategy contract requires and guarantees no partial/duplicate commit (a failed attempt's transaction rolls back via `IDbContextTransaction`'s dispose-without-commit semantics before the next attempt begins). The EXISTING public `BeginTransactionAsync()` gains a proactive guard — `DbContext.Database.CreateExecutionStrategy().RetriesOnFailure` (a genuine public EF Core API for exactly this check) — throwing a platform-specific, actionable `InvalidOperationException` directing the caller to `ExecuteInTransactionAsync` BEFORE EF Core's own native (less actionable) exception would otherwise fire. This guard is unconditional — it queries live EF Core state, independent of whether `.WithTransientFaultRetry()` (D-75) was ever called — so it correctly protects any consumer who enabled retry via `UsePostgreSQL(...)` directly. XML docs must state plainly: code running inside the `operation` delegate may execute MORE THAN ONCE under retry — it must be safe to re-run (the same general constraint EF Core's own retrying execution strategy already imposes platform-wide, not a new constraint this wrapper introduces) | WO-051 (P-320) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions`, `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |

| D-78 | Design `SharedKernelDbContext`'s pooling-safe user-context redesign (P-322, part 1) — **confirmed against the real shipped source** (`SharedKernelDbContext.cs`'s actual 9-parameter constructor — see D-83's correction note — and `AuditInterceptor.cs`'s `private readonly IUserContext _userContext` field, set once in its own constructor): under `AddDbContext` (scoped, today's only registration path), a NEW `AuditInterceptor`/`SoftDeleteInterceptor` instance is constructed per DI scope, so `_userContext` is correctly per-request. Net effect: in a long-running service with a single DI container and multi-tenancy enabled WITHOUT `.WithEncryption()` (whose own cache-key-widening incidentally, and expensively, masks this by forcing a model rebuild whenever the scoped `IEncryptionVersionOverride` instance differs), the tenant filter closure is built ONCE, from whichever `ITenantProvider` instance happened to construct the very first `TenantedDbContext` in the process, and is REUSED by the cached model for the life of the process — an existing, real cross-tenant data-exposure risk this design corrects as a byproduct of the pooling fix, not merely a pooling-only concern. This closes both the pooling hazard (D-80) and the pre-existing non-pooled staleness risk in the one fix — validated empirically, not just asserted, by a dedicated non-pooled regression test (T-88) proving two sequential same-subclass instances with different providers correctly isolate even though the model is cached across them | WO-051 (P-322) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |

| D-97 | Design `EfRepository<TAggregate, TId>.RestoreAsync` (P-337) — implements `IRestorableRepository<TAggregate,TId>` (D-96) UNCONDITIONALLY on `EfRepository<TAggregate,TId>` (exactly mirroring how `IBulkMutationRepository<TAggregate,TId>` is already implemented unconditionally there, regardless of whether `TAggregate` happens to be soft-deletable) — no separate base class. Body: `if (aggregate is not ISoftDeletable) throw new InvalidOperationException($"'{typeof(TAggregate).Name}' does not implement ISoftDeletable; RestoreAsync has no meaning for a non-soft-deletable aggregate.")`; otherwise attaches the aggregate if `Detached` (reusing the EXISTING `MarkAsModifiedIfDetached` helper so the entry ends up `Modified`), then writes `context.Entry(aggregate).CurrentValues[nameof(ISoftDeletable.IsDeleted)] = false`, `CurrentValues[nameof(ISoftDeletable.DeletedOn)] = null`, `CurrentValues[nameof(ISoftDeletable.DeletedBy)] = null` — the SAME `ChangeTracker.Entry(entity).CurrentValues[propertyName]`-only mutation rule that already governs `AuditInterceptor`/`SoftDeleteInterceptor`, extended here to repository-level restore code for the identical reason (no direct property setters on aggregates). `RestoreAsync` only STAGES the change (consistent with every other `EfRepository` write method's "stages mutations without committing" contract) — a subsequent `IUnitOfWork.SaveChangesAsync()` call persists it; because the entry is a normal `Modified` row at that point, `AuditInterceptor` sets `ModifiedBy`/`ModifiedOn` automatically with ZERO changes needed to either platform interceptor — restore genuinely goes through "the same interceptor/audit/domain-event pipeline a normal update would," per the phase's own acceptance criterion, entirely as a byproduct of this design, not extra plumbing | WO-053 (P-337) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-98 | Design `EfCorePersistenceBuilder.WithCommandTimeout(int commandTimeoutSeconds)` (P-337) — resolved in favor of `EfCorePersistenceBuilder` (EfCore package), NOT the PostgreSQL package's `UsePostgreSQL(...)`, because `DbContextOptionsBuilder.CommandTimeout(int?)` is a `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.RelationalDbContextOptionsBuilderExtensions` member — a PROVIDER-NEUTRAL relational EF Core concept already reachable via the `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Relational` package this domain already references, unlike `EnableRetryOnFailure` (P-320), which genuinely IS an Npgsql-only extension method with no relational-neutral equivalent. Implementation: wraps the caller-supplied `configureDb` action to ALSO call `optionsBuilder.CommandTimeout(commandTimeoutSeconds)` — the exact same wrapping pattern `.WithCompiledModel(IModel)` already uses for `.UseModel(compiledModel)`. No new `TransientFaultRetryOptions`-style DI-registered options POCO is introduced — unlike retry, nothing else in this domain needs to QUERY the configured timeout value at runtime the way `EfTransactionalUnitOfWork` queries `Database.CreateExecutionStrategy().RetriesOnFailure`, so a bare wrapping method fully satisfies the phase's "explicit, opt-in command-timeout configuration value" acceptance criterion with no companion DI-pairing method. Omitting the call preserves today's provider-default timeout behavior exactly | WO-053 (P-337) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-99 | Design `EfCorePersistenceBuilder.WithReadReplica(Action<DbContextOptionsBuilder> configureReplicaDb)` (P-338) — registers a KEYED singleton `DbContextOptions<TContext>` (key: a new `PersistenceWellKnown.ReadReplicaKey` constant, colocated per the Magic String convention) built from a fresh, independent `DbContextOptionsBuilder<TContext>().Options` after applying `configureReplicaDb` — the caller's own lambda is where the Npgsql-specific replica connection string is supplied (e.g. `optionsBuilder => optionsBuilder.UseNpgsql(replicaConnectionString)`), mirroring the EXISTING `AddSharedKernelEfCore<TContext>(Action<DbContextOptionsBuilder> configureDb)` pattern exactly — `EfCorePersistenceBuilder` itself never references Npgsql. Also registers a SCOPED `IReadReplicaContextAccessor<TContext>` (D-100) whose implementation constructs the replica `TContext` instance LAZILY (once per DI scope, cached thereafter — never per-call) via `ActivatorUtilities.CreateInstance<TContext>(serviceProvider, replicaOptions)`, deliberately REUSING the SAME scope-ambient, DI-resolved `AuditInterceptor`/`SoftDeleteInterceptor`/`ConcurrencyInterceptor` instances (and, transitively, their live `CurrentUserContext` per WO-051/P-322) that the PRIMARY `TContext` instance for this scope already resolved — so audit-field consistency between primary and replica reads is automatic, zero extra plumbing. Purely opt-in — omitting this call leaves `IReadReplicaContextAccessor<TContext>` unregistered entirely, and every existing consumer's read/write behavior is provably unchanged (D-101 falls back to the primary context whenever the accessor is absent) | WO-053 (P-338) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-100 | Design `IReadReplicaContextAccessor<TContext>` (P-338, internal to `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` — a wiring detail, not a consumer-facing abstraction) — single member `TContext GetEffectiveContext(TContext primaryContext)`: returns `primaryContext` UNCONDITIONALLY whenever `primaryContext.Database.CurrentTransaction != null` (an explicit EF Core transaction is currently open on the primary — the phase's own hard "reads inside an active transaction are never routed to the replica" rule, enforced by checking live EF Core transaction state, the SAME class of unconditional live-state check `EfTransactionalUnitOfWork`'s retry guard (WO-051/P-320) already uses); otherwise returns the lazily-constructed, scope-cached replica `TContext` instance (D-99). When `.WithReadReplica(...)` was never called, `IReadReplicaContextAccessor<TContext>` is simply never registered in DI — `EfReadRepository` (D-101) resolves it as `null` via the optional constructor parameter and always falls back to `primaryContext`, so default (no-replica) behavior is provably byte-for-byte unchanged from today with zero new code path exercised | WO-053 (P-338) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-101 | Design `EfReadRepository<TAggregate,TId>`'s optional read-replica constructor extension (P-338) — new OPTIONAL, nullable, DEFAULTING-to-`null` trailing constructor parameter `IReadReplicaContextAccessor<TContext>? replicaAccessor = null` (purely additive — every EXISTING `EfReadRepository` subclass across every consuming service continues to compile and behave identically without passing anything new). Every existing method body's `DbContext.Set<TAggregate>()`/`_evaluator.GetQuery(...)` call site is redirected through a new private computed property `EffectiveContext => replicaAccessor?.GetEffectiveContext(DbContext) ?? DbContext` — resolved AFRESH on every call, never cached across calls on the same repository instance, because transaction state can legitimately change between two read calls issued against the SAME injected repository within one logical request (e.g., a caller opens `ITransactionalUnitOfWork.BeginTransactionAsync()` between two `ListAsync` calls). `IRepository<TAggregate,TId>`/`EfRepository<TAggregate,TId>` (the write side) are completely untouched by this phase — writes always target the primary connection unconditionally, with no accessor parameter of any kind. XML docs and the package README must state, IN CAPITALS, that read-after-write consistency becomes the CALLER's responsibility once replica routing is enabled — a handler that writes then immediately reads via `IReadRepository` in the same logical operation may observe stale data under replication lag | WO-053 (P-338) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-102 | Design `VectorDistanceMetric` enum (P-339, `SharedKernel.Persistence.PostgreSQL/Vector/`) — `Cosine` \| `L2`, deliberately mirroring the exactly-two distance functions `Pgvector.EntityFrameworkCore`'s own `VectorExtensions` exposes (`CosineDistance`/`L2Distance`) — no third `DotProduct`/`InnerProduct` member, since the phase's own acceptance criteria scope this helper to "both cosine distance and L2/Euclidean distance, matching Pgvector.EntityFrameworkCore's own exposed distance functions," nothing more | WO-053 (P-339) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.PostgreSQL` | `●` |
| D-103 | Design `VectorOrderingExpressions.ByDistance<TAggregate>(Expression<Func<TAggregate,Vector>> vectorSelector, Vector queryVector, VectorDistanceMetric metric)` → `Expression<Func<TAggregate,object>>` (P-339, `SharedKernel.Persistence.PostgreSQL/Vector/`) — builds the SAME boxed-to-`object` key-selector expression shape `ISpecification<T>.OrderBy`/`OrderByDescending` already store (matching the existing `KeysetSpecification<T,TKey>` precedent for this exact boxing convention), whose body wraps `vectorSelector`'s member access in an `Expression.Call` invoking `Pgvector.EntityFrameworkCore.VectorExtensions.CosineDistance`/`.L2Distance` (chosen by `metric`) against `Expression.Constant(queryVector)`. The `MethodInfo` for each distance function is captured via a STATICALLY-TYPED delegate cast — `((Func<Vector,Vector,double>)VectorExtensions.CosineDistance).Method` — resolved entirely by the C# COMPILER, never via `Type.GetMethod`/`MakeGenericMethod` at runtime, satisfying the platform-wide reflection-elimination rule with zero exception needed. Deliberately does NOT call `AddOrderBy`/`ApplyOrderBy` itself and introduces NO new `Specification<T>` base class — the returned expression is designed to be passed by the CONSUMER's own `Specification<TAggregate>` subclass into ITS OWN protected `AddOrderBy` call from within that subclass's constructor (exactly how `KeysetSpecification<T,TKey>`/`PagedSpecification<T>` already populate protected members from their own constructors) — so this helper composes automatically with the consumer's own `Criteria`/`Includes`/`Take` with ZERO `SpecificationEvaluator<T>` pipeline changes, since nothing about the evaluator's existing OrderBy→...→Take handling changes. Scoped to `Vector`-typed properties only — a `float[]`-typed vector column (the OTHER type `HasVectorColumn` supports) is out of scope for this specific helper, a documented limitation, not a defect | WO-053 (P-339) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.PostgreSQL` | `●` |

| D-104 | Design `CurrencyValueConverter : ValueConverter<Currency, string>` (P-440, `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore/Conversions/`) — to-provider direction via `Currency`'s existing `SingleValueObject<TValue>` implicit operator to `string` (`(string)currency`, zero reflection); from-provider direction via the public `Currency.Create(code) → Result<Currency>` factory (P-310), unwrapping `.Value` and throwing a clear `InvalidOperationException` on an unexpected failure (a stored value is assumed already-validated at write time). This is a DELIBERATE REFINEMENT of P-440's own framing ("mirrors the existing `StronglyTypedIdValueConverter<TId,TValue>` precedent") — `Currency`/`Money` derive from `ValueObject`/`SingleValueObject<TValue>`, which already expose a public `Create` factory (03.Domain P-310), so reconstruction never needs `StronglyTypedIdValueConverter`'s reflection-located-constructor + compiled-`Expression.New` technique at all; that technique exists only because `StronglyTypedId<TValue>` (a different base hierarchy) has no such factory. Depends on `03.Domain`'s P-439 shipping `Currency.Create` | WO-066 (P-440) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-105 | Design `MoneyEntityTypeBuilderExtensions.OwnsMoney<TEntity>(EntityTypeBuilder<TEntity> builder, Expression<Func<TEntity,Money>> propertyExpression, string? amountColumnName = null, string? currencyColumnName = null)` (P-440) — configures a `Money`-typed property as an EF Core OWNED TYPE with two independently queryable columns: `Amount` (`decimal`, a single fixed precision/scale wide enough for every ISO 4217 minor-unit case — `decimal(19,4)` default, documented as safe headroom since `Money`'s own construction-time rounding already guarantees no more than `Currency.MinorUnitDigits` significant fractional digits) and `Currency` (`string` `HasMaxLength(3)`, mapped via `CurrencyValueConverter` from D-104). Column names default from the property name, overridable — mirrors the existing optional-column-name-override convention already used by `.Encrypt()`/`HasJsonbColumn`. A two-column owned-type mapping is chosen over a single packed-string column specifically so `Amount`/`Currency` remain independently filterable/aggregable in SQL (`WHERE Currency = 'USD' AND Amount > ...`, `SUM(Amount) WHERE Currency = 'USD'`) — a near-universal real-world requirement for monetary data that a packed-string encoding would foreclose | WO-066 (P-440) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-106 | Design and resolve `Money`'s owned-type MATERIALIZATION risk (P-440) — per `03.Domain/CLAUDE.md`'s locked shape, `Money`'s private constructor takes `(decimal amount, Currency currency, RoundingPolicy roundingPolicy)`, but only `Amount`/`Currency` are EF-Core-mapped, persisted properties; `RoundingPolicy` has no column, so EF Core's default constructor-binding convention cannot be trusted to resolve it without verification against the real (not-yet-implemented-as-of-this-design-pass) shipped type. Two candidate resolutions recorded for Core-phase decision: **(a) PREFERRED** — mirror `StronglyTypedIdValueConverter`'s own reflection-located-constructor + compiled-`Expression.New` technique (an already-accepted, already-AOT-documented pattern in this exact package) to explicitly supply `RoundingPolicy.BankersRounding` as a literal third constructor argument during owned-type materialization — safe because re-rounding an already-rounded stored value to the same precision is idempotent, and this preserves the independently-queryable two-column mapping from D-105; **(b) FALLBACK ONLY** — if (a) proves unworkable against `Money`'s real constructor once P-439 ships, collapse to a single packed-string `ValueConverter<Money,string>` (`"{amount}:{currencyCode}"`, reconstructed via the public `Money.Create(amount, Currency.Create(code).Value).Value`, zero reflection) at the DOCUMENTED cost of losing independent SQL-level filtering/aggregation on `Amount`/`Currency`. Core-phase implementer records which path was taken and why | WO-066 (P-440) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-107 | Design `ValueObjectOwnershipBuilder`'s scan to EXCLUDE `Money`-typed properties (P-440) — a `Money` property must always be configured explicitly via `.OwnsMoney(...)` (D-105), never silently auto-owned by the generic `IValueObject` scan with a wrong-precision default `decimal` column; mirrors the existing "anything precision/security-sensitive is opt-in-only" philosophy already applied to `.Encrypt()` | WO-066 (P-440) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-108 | Design removal of `EncryptedValueConverter`'s direct `IEncryptionKeyProvider` dependency (P-448) — SOURCE-VERIFIED against the real, currently-shipped `EncryptedValueConverter.cs`/`AesGcmEncryptionService.cs`: the decrypt-path pre-check (`keyProvider.GetKey(version) is null → throw EncryptionKeyNotFoundException`) is REDUNDANT with `ISymmetricEncryptionService.Decrypt`'s own `Result<byte[]>` failure surface — `AesGcmEncryptionService.Decrypt` already distinguishes `CryptographyErrorCodes.UnknownKeyId` (unresolvable key) from `CryptographyErrorCodes.DecryptionFailed` (tamper/wrong-key) as two DIFFERENT error codes, which is exactly the distinction the pre-check exists to preserve. New design: `EncryptedValueConverter`'s constructor DROPS the `IEncryptionKeyProvider keyProvider` parameter entirely; `Decrypt` calls `symmetricEncryptionService.Decrypt(payload)` directly and maps `result.Error?.Code == CryptographyErrorCodes.UnknownKeyId` → `EncryptionKeyNotFoundException(version)`, any other failure → the existing generic `CryptographicException` tamper message. This means the EF Core `ValueConverter` pipeline NEVER touches `IEncryptionKeyProvider` — sync or async — directly again. This is the HONEST resolution to P-448's stated "central design problem": the synchronous pipeline stays solvable not by blocking on async anywhere in `06.Persistence`, but by removing a redundant direct call whose only purpose (a friendlier exception type) is already available from `ISymmetricEncryptionService`'s existing `Result<T>` surface. `ISymmetricEncryptionService.Encrypt`/`.Decrypt` themselves remain synchronous per `01.Core`'s own already-locked P-446 design (`01.Core/CLAUDE.md`: sync members "retained... bridge to the async `IEncryptionKeyProvider` via `.GetAwaiter().GetResult()`" — `01.Core`'s own documented, accepted cost, explicitly OUT of `06.Persistence`'s jurisdiction and stated by P-446 itself to be "what keeps `06.Persistence`'s structurally-synchronous-only EF Core `ValueConverter` pipeline solvable in its own follow-on phase") | WO-068 (P-448) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-109 | Design `EncryptionModelConvention`'s corresponding simplification (P-448) — drops resolving `IEncryptionKeyProvider?` from DI (no longer needed by `EncryptedValueConverter` per D-108); constructor loses the `keyProvider` parameter; the two-branch converter-construction logic (configured vs. pass-through) now branches on `_symmetricEncryptionService is not null` alone, dropping the `&& _keyProvider is not null` half of today's condition | WO-068 (P-448) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-110 | Design `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider`/`NullEncryptionKeyProvider` migration to `01.Core`'s new async `IEncryptionKeyProvider` shape (P-448) — `GetCurrentKeyAsync(CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<CryptographicKey>` / `GetKeyAsync(string keyId, CancellationToken ct = default) → ValueTask<CryptographicKey?>`. SOURCE-VERIFIED: neither implementation performs genuine I/O today — `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider` reads an already-loaded `IOptionsMonitor<EncryptionOptions>.CurrentValue` plus the existing synchronous `EncryptionKeyByteCache` decode-once cache, pure in-memory/CPU work — so both wrap trivially in an already-completed `ValueTask.FromResult(...)`. XML docs must state explicitly, in contrast with a genuinely network-bound provider (e.g. the future `SharedKernel.Cryptography.KeyVault.Azure`, P-447): `AesGcmEncryptionService`'s internal `.GetAwaiter().GetResult()` bridge (`01.Core`'s own documented cost) NEVER actually blocks a thread on real I/O when THIS config-based provider is registered, since the returned `ValueTask` is already complete at the point it's awaited. `NullEncryptionKeyProvider.GetCurrentKeyAsync` still throws the same `InvalidOperationException` synchronously (before constructing any `ValueTask`, not via a faulted one) — behavior-preserving relative to today's sync `GetCurrentKey()` | WO-068 (P-448) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-111 | Confirm `EncryptionKeyByteCache` needs NO change (P-448) — it performs Base64 decoding only (CPU, never I/O), called synchronously from within `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider`'s now-`ValueTask`-wrapped methods exactly as it is called today from the sync methods | WO-068 (P-448) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-112 | Confirm `EncryptionRotationService`/`IEncryptionRotationJob` need NO direct call-site change (P-448) — grep-verified against the real source: neither calls `IEncryptionKeyProvider.GetKey`/`GetCurrentKey` directly; rotation operates entirely through `IEncryptionVersionOverride` plus triggering the (unchanged, still-synchronous) `EncryptedValueConverter`/`ISymmetricEncryptionService` pipeline via `SaveChangesAsync`. Since rotation already runs in an async hosted-service/job context, any FUTURE genuine need to call the async `IEncryptionKeyProvider` directly from rotation code remains trivially `await`-able there — no synchronous-pipeline constraint applies outside the `ValueConverter` itself | WO-068 (P-448) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-113 | Design `AuditRecord` (sealed record, P-456, new `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions/Auditing/` folder) — ALL properties `init`-only, no update/delete surface anywhere on the type, structurally: `Id` (Guid), `TenantId` (Guid, `Guid.Empty` sentinel mirrors `ITenantProvider`'s existing convention), `ActorId` (string — REUSES the platform's existing audit-string-format convention from `AuditInterceptor`: `userId.ToString("D")` or a service-name fallback, rather than inventing a new Guid-based actor shape), `Action` (string, caller-defined verb/code), `ResourceType` (string), `ResourceId` (string — caller stringifies, since aggregate PK types vary: `Guid`, `StronglyTypedId<T>`, `int`), `OccurredOn` (DateTimeOffset, `IClock`-sourced by the WRITER, never caller-supplied — prevents forged timestamps), `BeforeSnapshot`/`AfterSnapshot` (string?, OPAQUE — caller pre-serializes, this package never parses/diffs them, mirrors `IIdempotencyResponseStore`'s "store persists what it's handed" precedent), `CorrelationId` (string?), `ApprovalId` (string?, optional maker-checker linkage to `IRequiresDualApproval`/P-380), `RecordHash` (string, hex SHA-256 via `IContentHasher`), `PreviousRecordHash` (string?, null only for the first record in its (TenantId,ResourceType) chain) | WO-071 (P-456) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions` | `●` |
| D-114 | Design `AuditEntry` (sealed record, P-456) — the CALLER-SUPPLIED input to `IAuditTrailWriter.RecordAsync`, deliberately a SMALLER shape than `AuditRecord`: `Action`, `ResourceType`, `ResourceId`, `BeforeSnapshot`, `AfterSnapshot`, `CorrelationId`, `ApprovalId` only. `Id`/`TenantId`/`ActorId`/`OccurredOn`/`RecordHash`/`PreviousRecordHash` are ALL resolved/computed by the writer implementation, never caller-supplied — closes off "a caller fabricates its own audit trail" structurally rather than by convention | WO-071 (P-456) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions` | `●` |

No update/delete member exists on this interface AT ALL — the structural (not conventional) immutability guarantee the phase requires. Actor/tenant identity resolved internally by the implementation via the new `IAuditActorContext` seam (D-116); `OccurredOn` via `IClock`; hash chaining scoped per (TenantId, ResourceType) partition — never globally, so unrelated resources' audit streams never serialize against each other | WO-071 (P-456) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions` | `●` |

CORRECTS a slight imprecision in P-456's own root phase text ("06.Persistence's layering ceiling does not include 12.Security"): `06.Persistence` in fact already holds an APPROVED, narrowly-scoped exception referencing `SharedKernel.Security.Abstractions` directly (P-078/WO-014) — but that grant is scoped to `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` specifically, NOT to `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions`, where `IAuditTrailWriter`/`IAuditQueryService` live. Extending the EfCore-only exception to Abstractions would be an unjustified, un-recorded widening — hence a fresh local seam here, mirroring `05.Application`'s `IAuthorizationContext` bridge pattern in SHAPE (a package-local seam standing in for a real identity source) even though the two packages differ in WHY they need one (05.Application has zero Security.Abstractions access of any kind; 06.Persistence.Abstractions specifically has never been granted the exception its own EfCore sibling already has). | D-121 | Design `AuditRecordEntityConfiguration : IEntityTypeConfiguration<AuditRecord>` (P-457) — `HasKey(x => x.Id)`; `BeforeSnapshot`/`AfterSnapshot` mapped as plain `text` columns (opaque — never `jsonb`, since this package must never parse/validate caller-supplied snapshot content per D-113/P-456's explicit "opaque" requirement); `RecordHash`/`PreviousRecordHash` as `varchar(64)` (hex SHA-256 digest length); composite indexes `(TenantId, ResourceType, ResourceId, OccurredOn)` and `(TenantId, ActorId, OccurredOn)` for the two named access patterns | WO-071 (P-457) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-122 | Design `EfAuditTrailWriter : IAuditTrailWriter` (P-457) — constructor-injects `SharedKernelDbContext`, `IClock`, `IContentHasher` (from `01.Core/SharedKernel.Cryptography` — a NEW project reference for `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore`, since `IContentHasher` has not previously been consumed by this package), and `IAuditActorContext`. `RecordAsync` is SELF-CONTAINED and calls `SaveChangesAsync` ITSELF, immediately, rather than staging the new `AuditRecord` into the ambient `DbContext`/`IUnitOfWork` alongside unrelated business-entity changes — a deliberate design decision: hash-chain correctness requires reading the latest `PreviousRecordHash` for the (TenantId, ResourceType) partition and committing atomically against it, and audit correctness must never be contingent on whether/when the caller's OWN unrelated `IUnitOfWork.SaveChangesAsync()` eventually fires. Computes `RecordHash` via `IContentHasher.ComputeHashHex(...)` over a canonical byte representation of the new record's own fields (a documented, exact serialization order shared with D-124's verification routine via a common internal `AuditRecordHasher` helper, so write-time and verify-time hashing can never drift apart), chained to the immediately-prior record's `RecordHash` within the same (TenantId, ResourceType) partition. Assigns `Id` via `Guid.CreateVersion7()` (a plain BCL static call, mirroring `01.Core`'s `IIdGenerator`/`UuidV7IdGenerator` convention for a time-ordered, B-tree-friendly PK, without taking a dependency on that opt-in abstraction itself) | WO-071 (P-457) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-123 | Design `AuditRecordImmutabilityInterceptor : SaveChangesInterceptor` (P-457) — a FOURTH interceptor (alongside Audit/SoftDelete/Concurrency), registered ONLY when the audit-trail feature is opted into (`.WithAuditTrail()`, D-125), inspecting `ChangeTracker.Entries<AuditRecord>()` in `SavingChanges`/`SavingChangesAsync` and THROWING a new `AuditRecordImmutableException` (extends `SharedKernelException`) if ANY entry's `EntityState` is `Modified` or `Deleted` — the phase's LOAD-BEARING piece: structural immutability enforced at the ORM boundary, not left to convention. XML docs additionally recommend, IN CAPITALS, a DB-level `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE` grant on the underlying table as defense-in-depth, mirroring the existing encryption/soft-delete documentation style in this package. `EfAuditTrailWriter` (D-122) calls `SaveChangesAsync` on the SAME `DbContext` this interceptor is registered on, so the interceptor fires on every audit write too — harmless, since a freshly-`Added` `AuditRecord` is never `Modified`/`Deleted` | WO-071 (P-457) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-124 | Design `EfAuditQueryService : IAuditQueryService` (P-457) — built DIRECTLY on `ISpecificationEvaluator<AuditRecord>` + `SharedKernelDbContext.Set<AuditRecord>()` per D-120's refinement (NOT `IReadRepository<AuditRecord,Guid>`, since `AuditRecord` deliberately does not implement `IAggregateRoot<Guid>`). `GetResourceHistoryAsync`/`GetActorActionsAsync` call `evaluator.GetQuery(dbSet.AsQueryable(), spec)` then materialize — reusing the SAME keyset-seek-predicate translation `SpecificationEvaluator<T>` already provides for any `KeysetSpecification<T,TKey>` (P-317/WO-051), zero new query-translation code needed. `VerifyChainIntegrityAsync` streams the (tenant, resource-type) partition ordered by `(OccurredOn, Id)` across the given range and, for each record, recomputes its `RecordHash` via the SAME `AuditRecordHasher` helper D-122's writer uses, checking it against both the STORED `RecordHash` (detects a mutated record) and the PRIOR record's stored `RecordHash` (detects a missing/reordered link) — returns the first break found, or `IsIntact = true` after checking every record in range | WO-071 (P-457) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-125 | Design `EfCorePersistenceBuilder.WithAuditTrail()` (P-457) — opt-in builder method registering `EfAuditTrailWriter`/`EfAuditQueryService`/`AuditRecordImmutabilityInterceptor` and applying `AuditRecordEntityConfiguration` to the model; registers a DEFAULT `IAuditActorContext` implementation, `EfCoreAuditActorContext`, bridging the ALREADY-APPROVED `IUserContext`/`ITenantProvider` (P-078, `SharedKernel.Security.Abstractions`, already referenced by THIS package specifically — see D-116's correction) — `ActorId` computed via the SAME `userId.ToString("D")`/service-name-fallback format `AuditInterceptor` already uses (reuse, not reinvent), `TenantId` from `ITenantProvider.TenantId`. A consuming service MAY override this default with its own `IAuditActorContext` registration (last-registration-wins, mirroring the existing `IUserContext`/`ITenantProvider` placeholder-override pattern) — a genuine ergonomic advantage over `05.Application`'s `IAuthorizationContext`, whose owning package has NO Security.Abstractions access at all and can never ship a default, worth documenting explicitly | WO-071 (P-457) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-126 | **REFUTATION RECORD (P-498/WO-081, SEVERE).** The phase input's literal premise — "a `SavingChangesAsync`-time pre-warming hook makes `EncryptedValueConverter` safe against a KMS-backed `IEncryptionKeyProvider`" — is only half-true and, taken alone, is UNSOUND. Two independent reasons, both traced against the design-locked `01.Core` contracts (`SK.01.P491`/`SK.01.P492`), not assumed: **(1)** `SavingChangesAsync` (an `ISaveChangesInterceptor` hook) fires only inside `SaveChangesAsync` — a QUERY (`ToListAsync`/`FirstOrDefaultAsync`/any read) never calls `SaveChangesAsync` at all, so a write-only pre-warm hook does nothing whatsoever for the READ/materialization path, which is the path the motivating F1 defect actually describes ("every READ of an encrypted column becomes a blocking call... reading 100 rows means 200 blocking Key Vault calls"). **(2)** Even restricted to the write path, "warm the cache" does not, by itself, make the SYNCHRONOUS `ISymmetricEncryptionService.Encrypt`/`.Decrypt` calls `EncryptedValueConverter` must use (EF Core 10's `ValueConverter` has no async `ConvertToProviderExpression`/`ConvertFromProviderExpression` overload) legal to call at all: `EncryptionKeyProviderCapabilities.IsGenuinelySynchronous` (P-492) is a STATIC, provider-IDENTITY check — true only for a provider directly marked `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider`, or a `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` whose `.Inner` recursively satisfies the same check — computed ONCE at `AesGcmEncryptionService` construction and cached as a `bool`. Wrapping a KMS-backed provider in `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` (P-446, already shipped) does NOT flip this check to `true`, regardless of cache warmth, because `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` NEVER directly implements the marker and its `.Inner` (the real Azure/KMS provider) never can either — a KMS provider structurally cannot make the "genuinely, always, zero-I/O" claim the marker requires. So a bare `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`-wrapped KMS provider, warm cache or cold, makes EVERY sync `Encrypt`/`Decrypt` call throw `NotSupportedException`, unconditionally, forever — not a performance hazard any warming strategy can improve, a hard functional break. **Corrected design (D-127→D-133 below):** (a) the config-backed default gains the marker directly (closing the AC#5 regression this repack would otherwise cause for every EXISTING encryption user the moment `01.Core`'s P-492 ships upstream); (b) a NEW, `06.Persistence`-owned provider (`PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider`, D-129) HONESTLY earns the marker by construction — it never touches its wrapped inner provider from its own `IEncryptionKeyProvider` members, serving exclusively from an in-memory cache populated ONLY by explicit, asynchronous warm calls, and failing closed (non-blocking `null`/a clear synchronous exception) on a cache miss, exactly like today's already-handled "unknown key version" case; (c) warming is TWO hooks, not one — `ISaveChangesInterceptor.SavingChangesAsync` for writes (D-130) AND `IDbCommandInterceptor.ReaderExecutingAsync` for reads (D-130) — the latter is EF Core's genuine async pre-materialization extension point, distinct from `ValueConverter`, and is what actually closes the F1 defect's own literal scenario. Mirrors the `02.Caching`/P-497 precedent of recording a refuted premise explicitly rather than silently reshaping the brief | WO-081 (P-498) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-127 | Design `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider`/`NullEncryptionKeyProvider` gaining `: IEncryptionKeyProvider, ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider` (P-492's marker). Both are SOURCE-VERIFIED zero-I/O already (`EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider` reads an already-loaded `IOptionsMonitor.CurrentValue` plus the already-synchronous `EncryptionKeyByteCache`; `NullEncryptionKeyProvider` throws synchronously before constructing any `ValueTask`) — this is the SINGLE most load-bearing task in this phase: without it, every EXISTING config-backed `.WithEncryption()` user's sync `Encrypt`/`Decrypt` calls start throwing `NotSupportedException` the moment `01.Core`'s P-492 repack is picked up, since neither type currently implements the new marker. Satisfies AC#5 ("existing config-backed users see zero behavior change") | WO-081 (P-498) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-128 | Design associated-data (AAD) derivation for `EncryptedValueConverter`, closing `01.Core`'s own D-67 inventory item ("flagged as the hardest of the six... no direct row-PK access inside a vanilla EF Core `ValueConverter`"). Resolution: AAD is bound to the property's own STABLE STORAGE IDENTITY, not row content — `UTF8Bytes("{schema ?? "public"}.{table}.{column}")`, resolved via `IReadOnlyProperty.DeclaringEntityType.GetSchema()`/`.GetTableName()` + `IReadOnlyProperty.GetColumnName()` (EF Core relational metadata, already reachable) INSIDE `EncryptionModelConvention` at model-finalization time — ONE `EncryptedValueConverter` instance already exists per annotated property, so this is a fixed, constructor-time `byte[]` requiring no per-row access at all; reproducible at decrypt time by construction, since the same converter instance (same property) always supplies the same bytes. Weaker than row-level binding (a value copied to ANOTHER ROW of the SAME column still decrypts) but genuinely closes the real, valuable case P-491 asks for: a ciphertext spliced from one COLUMN into another (e.g. an encrypted SSN value pasted into an encrypted PhoneNumber column) now fails authentication instead of silently "decrypting" into garbage. **Documented hazard:** renaming a table/column carrying `.Encrypt()` changes its AAD and makes every existing row's ciphertext for that column permanently undecryptable (AAD mismatch, surfaces as the existing generic `CryptographicException` path — no new failure shape). Mitigation: extend the EXISTING `.Encrypt(bool? enabled = true)` extension with an optional `string? associatedDataOverride = null` parameter — when supplied, this stable caller-chosen string is used instead of the physical table/column name, giving rename-safety to teams that opt in; existing `.Encrypt()` callers are unaffected (additive optional parameter) | WO-081 (P-498) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-129 | Design `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider` (internal sealed class, `Encryption/`) — `: IEncryptionKeyProvider, ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider`. Ctor: `(IEncryptionKeyProvider inner, IEncryptionVersionOverride versionOverride)`. Internal state: `ConcurrentDictionary<string,byte[]> _warmCache` + `volatile string? _currentVersionTag`. `GetCurrentKeyAsync(ct)`: resolves the target tag as `versionOverride.OverrideVersion ?? _currentVersionTag`; if that tag is in `_warmCache`, returns an already-completed `ValueTask<CryptographicKey>`; if `_currentVersionTag` is still `null` (never warmed), throws `InvalidOperationException` SYNCHRONOUSLY before constructing any `ValueTask` — mirrors `NullEncryptionKeyProvider`'s existing synchronous-throw precedent — never blocks, never touches `inner`. `GetKeyAsync(keyId, ct)`: `_warmCache.TryGetValue(keyId, ...)`; a MISS returns an already-completed `ValueTask<CryptographicKey?>` wrapping `null` (never throws, per `IEncryptionKeyProvider`'s documented contract) — an unwarmed historical version surfaces EXACTLY like today's unknown/removed-key case, downstream unchanged (`EncryptionKeyNotFoundException` via the existing D-108 error-code branch). Two internal, ASYNC-ONLY methods — `WarmCurrentAsync(ct)` (awaits `inner.GetCurrentKeyAsync(ct)`, populates `_warmCache`, sets `_currentVersionTag`; no-op if already warm for the resolved tag) and `WarmVersionAsync(keyId, ct)` (awaits `inner.GetKeyAsync(keyId, ct)`, populates `_warmCache` if found; no-op if already warm) — callable ONLY from `EncryptionKeyPreWarmingInterceptor` (D-130) and the startup gate (D-133), NEVER from `GetCurrentKeyAsync`/`GetKeyAsync` themselves. This is what makes the `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider` claim HONEST (per P-492's "author-asserted, never inferred" rule) — the guarantee is enforced by construction (the sync-contract members literally cannot reach `inner`), not by hope or by cache-hit probability | WO-081 (P-498) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-130 | Design `EncryptionKeyPreWarmingInterceptor` (internal sealed class, `Interceptors/`) — `: ISaveChangesInterceptor, IDbCommandInterceptor`, a FIFTH interceptor, registered ONLY by `.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<TProvider>()` (D-131), never present in the default config-backed path. `SavingChangesAsync(...)`: if `ChangeTracker.Entries()` contains any `Added`/`Modified` entry whose entity type carries the `"SharedKernel:Encrypt"` annotation on ≥1 property (the SAME model-metadata scan `EncryptionRotationService`/`EncryptedEntityBatchProcessorRegistry` already use — zero reflection over CLR members), `await`s `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider.WarmCurrentAsync(ct)` BEFORE calling `base.SavingChangesAsync(...)` — this genuinely runs strictly before EF Core builds the command batches that invoke `ConvertToProviderExpression` (the sync `Encrypt` call site). `ReaderExecutingAsync(...)`: if `eventData.Context`'s model carries the `"SharedKernel:Encrypt"` annotation on ≥1 property anywhere (checked once per `DbContext` CLR type, cached — a coarse, whole-model gate, not per-query), `await`s `WarmCurrentAsync(ct)` BEFORE calling `base.ReaderExecutingAsync(...)` — this is EF Core's genuine ASYNC pre-materialization extension point (fires before `ExecuteReaderAsync` returns a `DbDataReader`, strictly before any row's `ConvertFromProviderExpression`/sync `Decrypt` call), fundamentally distinct from `ValueConverter`'s own no-async-path limitation, and is the piece that actually closes the F1 defect's literal READ scenario D-126 identified as uncovered by a write-only hook. Coarse-grained by design: warms whenever the model has ANY encrypted property, regardless of whether THIS specific query touches one — acceptable, since a warm no-op call is an O(1) dictionary check inside `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider`, not a KMS round trip | WO-081 (P-498) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-131 | Design `EfCorePersistenceBuilder<TContext>.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<TProvider>() where TProvider : class, IEncryptionKeyProvider` PLUS the structural DI-isolation fix that makes the SEVERE defect's actual root cause unreachable, not merely detected. **Root cause, confirmed against this package's own existing docs:** `.WithEncryption()` today registers `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider` as the UNKEYED `SharedKernel.Cryptography.IEncryptionKeyProvider`, and `01.Core`'s `AesGcmEncryptionService` (a container-wide singleton) resolves `IEncryptionKeyProvider` from the SAME unkeyed slot — meaning ANY other registration of that interface anywhere in the same `IServiceCollection` (e.g. `13.ServiceDefaults`'s `AddSharedKernelKeyVaultKeyProvider`, P-449, registering `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider`) silently wins or loses purely by registration ORDER, with NO domain boundary preventing one consumer's general-purpose KMS choice from also becoming the persistence layer's — exactly the mechanism the motivating F1 scenario describes. **Fix:** `.WithEncryption()` NEVER registers `IEncryptionKeyProvider`/`ISymmetricEncryptionService` unkeyed again, in EITHER mode. It constructs its OWN dedicated `ISymmetricEncryptionService` instance (mirroring `01.Core`'s own `AddSharedKernelCryptography()` wiring — same `AesGcmEncryptionService` concrete type, substituting only the `IEncryptionKeyProvider` argument) backed by `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider` (default mode) or the new `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider`-wrapped `TProvider` (external mode, this task), registered under a package-internal keyed-DI slot (a private sentinel key, never exposed publicly — no `01.Core`-registry-shaped constant needed since this is not a cross-domain wire contract). `EncryptionModelConvention`'s own DI registration is rewired to resolve THIS keyed instance exclusively — `EncryptedValueConverter`'s public constructor shape is UNCHANGED (still a plain `ISymmetricEncryptionService symmetricEncryptionService` parameter; only the DI WIRING that supplies it changes). `WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<TProvider>()` itself: resolves `TProvider` from the ambient/unkeyed container (e.g. an `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` a consuming service separately registers for its OWN general-purpose crypto, optionally already `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider`-wrapped — doesn't matter, only its async members are ever called), wraps it in `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider`, registers `EncryptionKeyPreWarmingInterceptor` (D-130), and MUST be called after `.WithEncryption(...)` — calling it first throws an actionable `InvalidOperationException` at `.Build()` time, mirroring the existing pooling+encryption/pooling+factory ordering guards. Because the ambient unkeyed slot is never touched by this package in either mode, accidental cross-purpose leakage between a service's general-purpose crypto and its persistence-layer column encryption becomes STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE, not merely fail-fast-detected | WO-081 (P-498) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-132 | Design the startup fail-fast validation extending `.WithEncryption()`'s EXISTING eager `EncryptionOptions` validation pass (unchanged trigger point) to additionally assert `EncryptionKeyProviderCapabilities.IsGenuinelySynchronous(...)` against whichever `IEncryptionKeyProvider` this package is ABOUT to wire into its own keyed `ISymmetricEncryptionService` (D-131) — throwing an actionable `InvalidOperationException` naming the two sanctioned paths (the config-backed default, or `.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<TProvider>()`) if it somehow fails. Given D-131's structural fix (D-127 marks the default provider, D-129 makes the external path's provider HONESTLY marked by construction), this check should be UNREACHABLE-FALSE on every code path this package itself wires — it is deliberately defense-in-depth for a hypothetical future regression (e.g. a maintainer bypassing the builder to hand-construct `EncryptionModelConvention` directly), not the primary defense mechanism. Also update the existing "`ISymmetricEncryptionService` must be resolvable" check: since this package no longer resolves the ambient unkeyed registration, the check instead verifies the INDIVIDUAL dependencies the persistence-scoped instance needs (e.g. `ISecureRandomGenerator`) are resolvable, still naming `AddSharedKernelCryptography()` in the failure message | WO-081 (P-498) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-133 | Design the one-time startup warm-up sequencing for external-provider mode — a blocking-at-BOOT-ONLY (never per-request) `await PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider.WarmCurrentAsync(...)` performed by a hosted-service-based readiness gate, mirroring `WithMigrationsOnStartup()`'s existing "block readiness until done" shape, registered automatically by `.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<TProvider>()`. Guarantees `_currentVersionTag` is populated before the host accepts traffic, so `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider.GetCurrentKeyAsync`'s synchronous-throw-if-unwarmed path (D-129) is reachable only under a genuine startup-ordering bug, never in normal operation — directly satisfies AC#2 ("does not block a thread-pool thread on a cache-cold Key Vault call under normal operation") for the write path, with the boot-time cost being a ONE-TIME, expected, non-thread-pool-pressured blocking wait, not a per-request one | WO-081 (P-498) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
| D-134 | **Non-goal record.** `IEncryptionRotationJob`/`EncryptionRotationService` remains config-backed-only (`EncryptionOptions.Keys`-driven, reading both `fromVersion`/`toVersion` from that dictionary) in this phase — explicitly OUT OF SCOPE. Orchestrating rotation against a KMS-backed provider (coordinating with `01.Core`'s `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider.MintNewVersionAsync`, `SK.01.P496`, itself still implementation-pending) is a materially different design problem — which versions exist, how "current" advances, how a batch job would pre-warm `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider` for a specific `fromVersion`/`toVersion` pair before its loop starts — left to a future, separately-dispatched phase. In external-provider mode today, `EncryptionRotationService` continues to function only insofar as the versions it needs happen to already be warm; an unwarmed historical version surfaces as the existing, already-handled `EncryptionKeyNotFoundException` per-row failure path (D-129), never a crash or a block | WO-081 (P-498) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |
