> **S-17 partial-completion note (Scaffold-phase pass, superseded — see closure note directly below):** the dead `Fixtures/PostgreSqlContainerFixture.cs` file was deleted outright (confirmed via grep it was never instantiated anywhere — only self-referenced within its own file); the now-empty `Fixtures/` folder was removed too. Deleting it broke the build via one stray unused `using SharedKernel.Persistence.PostgreSQL.Tests.Fixtures;` import in `Integration/PostgreSQLIntegrationTests.cs` (that file never actually used the fixture type — it rolls its own inline `PostgreSqlContainer`, exactly like `ConcurrencyIntegrationTests`/`KeysetPaginationIntegrationTests`/`TransientFaultRetryIntegrationTests`/`DapperReadServiceIntegrationTests` already do) — the dead import was removed, restoring a clean build. The `Testcontainers.PostgreSql` `PackageReference` was **deliberately left in place** on both `.csproj` files: all five remaining integration-test classes across both projects still directly instantiate `PostgreSqlBuilder`/`PostgreSqlContainer` inline (confirmed via repo-wide grep) — removing the package reference now would break the build. That migration onto `16.Testing`'s shared `PostgreSqlContainerFixture` via `[CollectionDefinition("PostgreSQL")]`/`IClassFixture<...>` is explicitly T-106–T-108 (Tests phase, not yet dispatched to this session) — this task is left `◐` In Progress, not `●` Complete, until those tasks land and the package-reference removal can be safely evaluated for real.
>
> **S-17 closure note (Scaffold-phase pass, later session):** completed the T-106–T-108 fixture migration in the same session that closed this task, rather than leaving it gated on a separate Tests-phase dispatch — added `[CollectionDefinition("PostgreSQL")] : ICollectionFixture<PostgreSqlContainerFixture>` (`Integration/PostgreSqlTestCollection.cs`); migrated `ConcurrencyIntegrationTests`/`KeysetPaginationIntegrationTests`/`TransientFaultRetryIntegrationTests` off their own inline containers onto the shared fixture, each targeting its own uniquely-named database (`sk_persistence_concurrency`/`sk_persistence_keyset`/`sk_persistence_transient_retry`) via `NpgsqlConnectionStringBuilder` — **a genuine correction to the original D-95 design**, discovered only by implementing it: `Database.EnsureCreatedAsync()` checks whether the target database has ANY tables at all, not specifically the calling model's own tables, so three different `DbContext` models sharing one literal database (as D-95 originally specified) would silently skip creating whichever model's schema ran second — per-class database names within the one shared container close this gap while still eliminating the redundant container starts. `PostgreSQLIntegrationTests` deliberately did **not** join the shared collection — its pgvector round-trip test requires the `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` image, which the shared fixture's plain `postgres:16.4` image cannot provide (the extension binary is simply absent from a vanilla PostgreSQL image) — it keeps its own dedicated container; only its obsolete parameterless-constructor-plus-`.WithImage(...)` call was fixed to `new PostgreSqlBuilder("pgvector/pgvector:pg16")`. `DapperReadServiceIntegrationTests` switched to `IClassFixture<PostgreSqlContainerFixture>`; writing this surfaced a second genuine bug, also only visible under real execution — its `DROP TABLE` order (parent `dapper_customer` before its dependents `dapper_order`/`dapper_payment`) only ever worked because each test method previously got a brand-new container, and once the database persists across test methods the second test method's `InitializeAsync` failed with Npgsql `PostgresException` `2BP01` ("cannot drop table ... because other objects depend on it") — fixed by reordering the drops child-before-parent. `Testcontainers.PostgreSql` `PackageReference` removed from `SharedKernel.Persistence.Dapper.Tests.csproj` (no longer directly referenced there); **left in place** on `SharedKernel.Persistence.PostgreSQL.Tests.csproj` since `PostgreSQLIntegrationTests.cs` still genuinely needs it for its dedicated pgvector container — this is a correct evaluation outcome per S-17's own conditional wording, not a shortcut. `dotnet build` clean (0 errors, all four pre-existing `CS0618` obsolete-constructor warnings gone) and `dotnet test` green against a real Docker daemon: `SharedKernel.Persistence.PostgreSQL.Tests` 33/33, `SharedKernel.Persistence.Dapper.Tests` 15/15. Repo-wide grep confirms zero remaining `postgres:16-alpine` or obsolete `new PostgreSqlBuilder()` references in any `.cs` source file in `06.Persistence`.

| C-85 | Implement `IBulkMutationRepository<TAggregate, TId>` interface and `EfRepository<TAggregate, TId>` set-based bulk mutation members (P-148) — new interface `IBulkMutationRepository<TAggregate, TId>` in `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore/Repositories/` (or `Specifications/`) with `Task<int> ExecuteUpdateAsync(ISpecification<TAggregate> spec, Expression<Func<SetPropertyCalls<TAggregate>, SetPropertyCalls<TAggregate>>> setPropertyCalls, CancellationToken ct = default)` and `Task<int> ExecuteDeleteAsync(ISpecification<TAggregate> spec, CancellationToken ct = default)`; `EfRepository<TAggregate, TId>` implements this interface as additional surface (alongside `IRepository<TAggregate, TId>`); both methods first call the new `BulkSpecificationGuard.Validate(spec)` (see C-86) to fail-fast on unsupported spec shapes, then build the base `IQueryable<TAggregate>` by applying only evaluator steps 0 (`IgnoreQueryFilters` when `spec.IncludeDeleted == true`) and 1 (`Criteria`) — do NOT call the full `SpecificationEvaluator`/`GetQuery` (which would apply Includes/OrderBy/Skip/Take, all rejected by the guard anyway); `ExecuteUpdateAsync` calls `.ExecuteUpdateAsync(setPropertyCalls, ct)`; `ExecuteDeleteAsync` calls `.ExecuteDeleteAsync(ct)` — issuing a hard physical `DELETE` regardless of `ISoftDeletable`; both return the affected row count; XML docs on both methods carry a prominent `<remarks>` warning that these bypass `SaveChanges`, all three platform interceptors (Audit/SoftDelete/Concurrency), and domain event dispatch entirely | WO-024 (P-148) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |

> **T-106/T-107 correction (closed alongside S-17, same session):** T-106/T-107's own task text ("all four classes switch to the shared fixture") does not hold — see the S-17 closure note above (Scaffold phase table) for the full reasoning: `PostgreSQLIntegrationTests` deliberately does NOT join `[CollectionDefinition("PostgreSQL")]`/the shared `PostgreSqlContainerFixture`, since its pgvector round-trip test needs the `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` image the shared fixture's plain `postgres:16.4` image cannot provide. Only `ConcurrencyIntegrationTests`/`KeysetPaginationIntegrationTests`/`TransientFaultRetryIntegrationTests` (three classes, not four) share the one `PostgreSqlContainerFixture` instance registered by `PostgreSqlTestCollection`, each targeting its own uniquely-named database (`sk_persistence_concurrency`/`sk_persistence_keyset`/`sk_persistence_transient_retry`) to avoid `Database.EnsureCreatedAsync()`'s coarse "does this database have ANY tables" check silently skipping a second/third model's schema creation. Every pre-existing `[Fact]`/`[Theory]` across all four `PostgreSQL.Tests` classes still passes unmodified in intent — the dead `Fixtures/PostgreSqlContainerFixture.cs` file was already confirmed absent (deleted at the earlier partial S-17 pass) — so both tasks' actual acceptance bar is met; only the "all four classes" framing in their own task text was inaccurate and is corrected here rather than silently rewritten.

| T-122 | **REWRITTEN 2026-09-02** (was: raw `WHERE Currency = 'USD' AND Amount > ...`-shaped SQL assertion proving two independently queryable columns — infeasible against the shipped design, see below). Money round-trip test (P-440) — zero-decimal (JPY), two-decimal (USD), three-decimal (BHD) currencies survive a full EF Core save+reload cycle with `Amount`/`Currency` both intact, via the shipped single packed-string column (D-106 fallback). A companion test makes the resulting capability loss concrete: a raw SQL query referencing `Currency`/`Amount` columns fails outright — they are NOT independently SQL-queryable, a genuine capability loss vs. the original D-105 two-column design, confirmed unreachable through any public EF Core 10 API (re-verified this session by reflecting over the real EF Core 10.0.10 assembly: `ITypeBase.ConstructorBinding` has no public setter anywhere; the only settable `ConstructorBinding`/`HasConstructorBinding(...)` surface lives entirely in `Metadata.Internal`/`Metadata.Runtime`, non-public and non-SemVer-covered) — worth arch-lead's attention as a follow-up decision, not a silent scope cut | WO-066 (P-440) | `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` | `●` |

## Phase: P-541 — Persistence: Attach the Domain Clock on Load and Persist the Event Sequence <!-- phase-key: SK.06.P541 -->

> **Origin:** root P-541, raised by `03.Domain` P-540 and required before `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` publishes. P-540 removed the `NullClock` sentinel, so an aggregate materialized by EF Core has no clock and throws the first time it raises a timestamped event or soft-deletes. No persistence test loaded an aggregate and then mutated it, which is why the suites stayed green.
> **Found by execution, recorded because it generalizes:** the first implementation added `new DomainClockMaterializationInterceptor(clock)` to each context. EF Core treats `IMaterializationInterceptor` as a singleton interceptor that keys its internal service provider, so every context built a new internal provider. Two `AuditTrailTests` caught it: the InMemory store is scoped to that provider, so the "attacker" context saw an empty database. The shipped design is one static instance that reads the clock from the context being materialized. **Any future `IMaterializationInterceptor`/`IInstantiationBindingInterceptor` must be a shared instance.**
> **Deferred, with reason:** stamping outgoing events with `Version` — `IDomainEvent` has no sequence member and `04.Contracts`' `EventEnvelope` no aggregate-version field, so there is no hook; it needs its own cross-domain design.
> Depends on: `03.Domain` P-540.

| ID | Task | Package(s) | State |
|----|------|-----------|:-----:|
| D-135 | Design attach-on-load: interceptor type, registration in both the non-pooled (`OnConfiguring`) and pooled paths, clock source without a new `SharedKernelDbContext` constructor parameter | SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore | `●` |
| C-173 | `DomainClockMaterializationInterceptor` (shared `FromContext` instance reading `SharedKernelDbContext.Clock`, plus a public `IClock` constructor for other contexts); `AuditInterceptor.Clock` and `SharedKernelDbContext.Clock` internal accessors; registered in `OnConfiguring` and the pooled factory | SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore | `●` |
| C-174 | `EntityTypeConfigurationBase` maps `IHasVersion.Version` as a required column, deliberately not a concurrency token | SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore | `●` |
| C-175 | P-540 migration: keyset evaluator unwraps IDs through `op_Explicit`; `StronglyTypedIdValueConverter`/`CurrencyValueConverter` read `.Value`/`.Code`; `MoneyValueConverter` consumes `ValidationResult<T>`; audit keyset contract test follows the tiebreak direction | SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore, SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions | `●` |
| T-147 | `DomainClockMaterializationInterceptorTests` (9): tracked and no-tracking loads attach the context clock; soft delete after load uses it; `Version` survives a reload and continues; mapping is required and not a concurrency token; an attached clock is kept; non-aggregates untouched; null clock rejected; **two contexts share one internal service provider**. Keyset: duplicate sort keys walk every row exactly once, ascending and descending. - [2026-09-08] C-166..C-172 → ● in SK.06.Core; T-139..T-146 → ● in SK.06.Tests; DO-69/DO-70 → ● in SK.06.Docs; P-13 → ● in SK.06.Published (WO-081/P-498, SEVERE, all 18 remaining tasks) — implemented against the corrected design D-126..D-134 already locked this session. `01.Core`'s `SK.01.P491`/`SK.01.P492` confirmed shipped on disk first. Shipped: `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider`/`NullEncryptionKeyProvider` implement `ISynchronousEncryptionKeyProvider` (D-127); `EncryptedValueConverter` gained required `byte[] associatedData`, derived by `EncryptionModelConvention` from table+column storage identity or an `associatedDataOverride` (D-128); new `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider` (D-129), `EncryptionKeyPreWarmingInterceptor` (D-130, dual write/read hooks), `EncryptionKeyPreWarmingHostedService` (D-133), `PersistenceEncryptionKeys` (internal keyed-DI slot names); `EfCorePersistenceBuilder.WithExternalEncryptionKeyProvider<TProvider>()` plus the keyed-DI structural isolation fix — `.WithEncryption()` never touches the ambient unkeyed `IEncryptionKeyProvider`/`ISymmetricEncryptionService` slot in either mode anymore (D-131); `EncryptionStartupValidator` rewritten to check the keyed slot + `IsGenuinelySynchronous` (D-132). Two corrections against the design sketch during implementation: `EncryptionKeyPreWarmingInterceptor`'s constructor takes only `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider` (no separate `IEncryptionVersionOverride` param — unnecessary, `WarmCurrentAsync()` resolves it internally); the D-132 startup check resolves a second keyed slot for `IEncryptionKeyProvider` itself rather than checking "individual dependencies like `ISecureRandomGenerator`" (source-verified `AesGcmEncryptionService` has only one constructor parameter). `SharedKernelDbContext`'s keyed-service resolution uses `DbContextOptions.FindExtension<CoreOptionsExtension>()?.ApplicationServiceProvider` — zero downstream `TContext` subclass signature changes required, no `[FromKeyedServices]` attribute dependency. Unplanned nice side effect: the config-backed default no longer needs `01.Core`'s `AddSharedKernelCryptography()` called at all. Three pre-existing rotation test files had an now-obsolete unkeyed `ISymmetricEncryptionService` registration removed. 8 new test files added under `Encryption/`; full `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore.Tests` suite 448/448 green (0 regressions); `dotnet pack` clean. `06.Persistence/CLAUDE.md` and `README.md` fully migrated off "design-locked/pending" language for P-498. `SK.06.Core`/`SK.06.Tests`/`SK.06.Docs`/`SK.06.Published` all 100% `●` — all four promoted to root; `SK.06.Design`/`SK.06.Scaffold` already `●`. Every `06.Persistence` phase key is now `●` (state-map-phase)
- [2026-09-08] P-498 (WO-081, SEVERE) processed — 9 new Design tasks added (D-126..D-134, all `●`, design-locked this session, mirroring the coordinated-wave precedent `01.Core`/`core-arch-planner` set for `SK.01.P491`/`SK.01.P492`/`SK.01.P496` earlier the same session) plus 18 `○` implementation tasks across Core (C-166..C-172), Tests (T-139..T-146), Docs (DO-69/DO-70), and Published (P-13). **Refuted the phase input's own literal premise (D-126), same discipline `02.Caching`/P-497 applied**: a `SavingChangesAsync`-only pre-warming hook cannot possibly close this defect — it never fires for a query (`SaveChanges` is never called on the read path, which is what the motivating F1 scenario actually describes: "every READ... 100 rows means 200 blocking Key Vault calls"), and even for writes, wrapping a KMS provider in `CachedEncryptionKeyProvider` (01.Core, already shipped) does NOT satisfy `01.Core`'s `SK.01.P492` `IsGenuinelySynchronous` gate regardless of cache warmth — that check is a static provider-IDENTITY test, not a per-call blocking test, and a KMS provider can never honestly claim it. Corrected design: (1) `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider`/`NullEncryptionKeyProvider` gain the marker directly, closing what would otherwise be a hard regression for every EXISTING config-backed user the moment `01.Core`'s P-492 ships (D-127); (2) a new `06.Persistence`-owned `PreWarmedEncryptionKeyProvider` HONESTLY earns the marker by construction — serves exclusively from an in-memory cache, never touches its wrapped inner provider from its own `IEncryptionKeyProvider` members, fails closed (non-blocking) on a miss (D-129); (3) TWO warming hooks, not one — `ISaveChangesInterceptor.SavingChangesAsync` for writes AND `IDbCommandInterceptor.ReaderExecutingAsync` for reads, the latter being EF Core's genuine async pre-materialization extension point and the piece that actually closes the F1 scenario (D-130); (4) a structural DI-isolation fix (D-131) that makes the SEVERE defect's actual root cause — `.WithEncryption()`'s `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider` and e.g. `13.ServiceDefaults`'s `AddSharedKernelKeyVaultKeyProvider`'s `AzureKeyVaultEncryptionKeyProvider` both registering the SAME unkeyed `IEncryptionKeyProvider`, order-dependent last-wins — structurally UNREACHABLE rather than merely fail-fast-detected: `.WithEncryption()` now always constructs its own keyed-DI-isolated `ISymmetricEncryptionService` instance, in both modes. Also solved `01.Core`'s own D-67 "hardest of the six" AAD-derivation open question (D-128): AAD is bound to the property's stable table+column storage identity (resolved once at model-finalization time, no row-PK access needed), with an optional `associatedDataOverride` escape hatch for rename-safety. Depends on `01.Core`'s `SK.01.P491`/`SK.01.P492` shipping past design-lock into Core before this phase's own Core tasks can compile — recorded as an explicit sequencing dependency, not yet a blocker since this session is design-only. Overall Progress: `SK.06.Design` 134/134 `●` (still promotable); `SK.06.Core`/`SK.06.Tests`/`SK.06.Docs`/`SK.06.Published` all moved from `●` to `◐` (7/8/2/1 new pending tasks respectively) — none promoted (persistence-arch-planner)

Key decisions — **P-147**: `EncryptionRotationService.LoadBatchAsync` reflection eliminated via non-generic `DbContext.Set(Type)` + non-generic `Queryable.Skip`/`Take`/`ToListAsync(IQueryable, ct)`; new `IEncryptionVersionOverride` scoped accessor (mutable `OverrideVersion`) injected into `EncryptedValueConverter` as an optional constructor parameter — `Encrypt` resolves target version as `versionOverride?.OverrideVersion ?? options.CurrentVersion`; `EncryptionRotationService.RotateAsync` sets the batch context's override to `toVersion` for the duration of `SaveChangesAsync` only, leaving `EncryptionOptions.CurrentVersion` untouched and unrelated concurrent scopes unaffected. **P-148**: new `IBulkMutationRepository<TAggregate, TId>` lives in EfCore (not Abstractions — `SetPropertyCalls<T>` is an EF Core type), implemented directly by `EfRepository<TAggregate, TId>`; `ExecuteUpdateAsync`/`ExecuteDeleteAsync` apply only evaluator steps 0 (conditional `IgnoreQueryFilters`) and 1 (`Criteria`) after passing a new `BulkSpecificationGuard` that throws `UnsupportedSpecificationException` (extends `SharedKernelException`) for any spec with `Includes`, `StringIncludes`, `OrderBy`/`OrderByDescending`/`ThenBys`, `Skip`, or `Take`; `ExecuteDeleteAsync` always issues a hard physical `DELETE` regardless of `ISoftDeletable`; both methods bypass `SaveChanges`, all three platform interceptors, and domain event dispatch — documented as a prominent hard-violation-adjacent warning. **P-149**: `IReadRepository<TAggregate, TId>` gains `StreamAsync`/`StreamProjectedAsync<TResult>` returning `IAsyncEnumerable<T>` (BCL type, zero new Abstractions dependency); `EfReadRepository` implements via `GetQuery`/`GetProjectedQuery` + unconditional `.AsNoTracking()` (the one documented exception to spec's `AsNoTracking` flag being honored) + `.AsAsyncEnumerable()`; Skip/Take honored as a row-window applied before streaming begins — no special-casing needed. **P-150**: new `DatabaseReadinessResult` sealed record (`IsHealthy`, `Latency`, `Provider`, `ErrorMessage?`) in `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions/Diagnostics/` (BCL only); `IDbConnectionFactory.CheckReadinessAsync` extension also in Abstractions (uses only `System.Data` types); `SharedKernelDbContext.CheckReadinessAsync` extension in EfCore via `Database.CanConnectAsync` + `Stopwatch`; no `IHealthCheck` implementation in `06.Persistence` — wrapping is a `13.ServiceDefaults` concern, documented as cross-reference only; no new `Microsoft.Extensions.Diagnostics.HealthChecks` dependency. **P-151**: new `IDataSeeder<TContext>` (single `SeedAsync` method, idempotency documented as an implementation contract) in EfCore; `EfCorePersistenceBuilder<TContext>` gains opt-in `.WithMigrationsOnStartup()` and `.AddSeeder<TSeeder>()`, both registering a single `MigrationAndSeedHostedService<TContext>` only when called; multi-replica race mitigation via PostgreSQL advisory lock (`pg_advisory_lock`/`unlock` through existing `IDbConnectionFactory`) — no new `02.Caching` reference; consuming services may alternatively wrap with `02.Caching.Redis.DistributedLocking` themselves (documented, not implemented here); explicitly not a migration-authoring tool and does not replace `WithCompiledModel`. - [2026-06-30] WO-037 (P-227, P-228) planned — two focused phases added: D-59..D-64 (Design: 6 tasks), S-13..S-14 (Scaffold: 2 tasks), C-93..C-97 (Core: 5 tasks), T-55..T-60 (Tests: 6 tasks), DO-37..DO-38 (Docs: 2 tasks); 21 new tasks total; no new Published tasks (no new packages; the existing four packages re-pack unaffected by these two phases). **P-227** — `EncryptedValueConverter`'s hand-rolled `AesGcm`/`RandomNumberGenerator` mechanism is replaced by delegation to `01.Core/SharedKernel.Cryptography`'s `ISymmetricEncryptionService`/`IEncryptionKeyProvider`/`EncryptedPayload`/`CryptographicKey` (direct `ProjectReference`, legal under existing layering, no exception needed — corrects a confirmed duplication of `01.Core`'s own hard rule against hand-rolled symmetric encryption). The on-disk wire format `"v{version}:{Base64(nonce||ciphertext||tag)}"` is preserved byte-for-byte — the converter keeps ownership of wire-format packing/parsing; only the cryptographic primitive moves. New internal `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider` implements `IEncryptionKeyProvider`, bridging `EncryptionOptions`/`IOptionsMonitor` (hot-reload preserved) and the existing rotation-scoped `IEncryptionVersionOverride` seam (P-147) — the override's `OverrideVersion ?? CurrentVersion` precedence now resolves inside `GetCurrentKey()` instead of inside the old hand-rolled `Encrypt` method, with no behavioral change to `EncryptionRotationService.RotateAsync`. `EncryptionKeyNotFoundException` is preserved as the public exception type; the converter pre-checks `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider.GetKey(parsedVersion)` before delegating to `ISymmetricEncryptionService.Decrypt`, throwing immediately on an unknown version rather than waiting for a `Result.Failure`. The root `CLAUDE.md` "What Goes Where" row distinguishing column encryption from general-purpose encryption is updated (cross-referenced at DO-37) to describe a *delegation* relationship instead of two independent implementations. **P-228** — `EfUnitOfWork` gains a second interface declaration, `SharedKernel.Application.Behaviors.IUnitOfWork`, satisfied by its existing `SaveChangesAsync` method body (both interfaces are structurally compatible); new direct `ProjectReference` from `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` to `05.Application/SharedKernel.Application.Behaviors` (legal: 06 may reference 01–05). New opt-in `EfCorePersistenceBuilder.WithApplicationTransactionBehavior()` fluent method registers the same scoped `EfUnitOfWork` instance against both `IUnitOfWork` interfaces, finally shipping as code the bridge that `05.Application/CLAUDE.md` had only documented as a "future work order" option (a) since WO-035. Omitting the new method leaves `Build()` behavior unchanged. Cross-domain dependency rows added for both `01.Core/SharedKernel.Cryptography` and `05.Application/SharedKernel.Application.Behaviors`; Overall Progress: Design 64 total/6○, Scaffold 14/2○, Core 97/5○, Tests 60/6○, Docs 38/2○ (persistence-arch-planner)

- [2026-07-30] WO-051 batch 2 (P-321..P-325) planned — the second and final batch of this work order: D-77..D-84 (Design: 8 tasks), C-118..C-128 (Core: 11 tasks), T-83..T-97 (Tests: 15 tasks), DO-47..DO-52 (Docs: 6 tasks), P-05..P-08 (Published: 4 tasks); 44 new tasks total. Package Board's `.Abstractions`/`.EfCore` rows corrected from stale "Current Phase: Core" to "Published" directly in this pass (both packages' own `P-01`/`P-02` have been `●` since WO-013 — a pure tracking-table fix, not deferred work). Key decisions — **P-321**: `DapperReadService` gains two multi-mapping `QueryAsync<TFirst,TSecond,TReturn>`/`<TFirst,TSecond,TThird,TReturn>` overloads (Dapper's `splitOn`-based join projection) and a `QueryMultipleAsync<TResult>(sql, Func<SqlMapper.GridReader,Task<TResult>> readFunc, ...)` grid-reader wrapper (connection stays open for the full `readFunc` duration, since `GridReader` streams sequential result sets over one connection); the private `_connectionFactory` field is promoted to `protected IDbConnectionFactory ConnectionFactory { get; }` as the documented extension seam, mirroring `TenantedDbContext.TenantProvider`'s existing protected-property precedent. **P-322** — the most consequential phase in this batch, verified against real shipped source rather than assumed: confirmed `AuditInterceptor`/`SoftDeleteInterceptor` capture `IUserContext` in their OWN constructors (correct today only because a fresh scoped instance is built per non-pooled request) and `TenantedDbContext.TenantProvider` is a get-only property whose value is baked into the compiled tenant-filter expression via `Expression.Constant(provider, ...)` at `OnModelCreating` time. Cross-referencing `EncryptionAwareModelCacheKeyFactory`'s own shipped XML doc — which states EF Core's default model cache is keyed only by `(context.GetType(), designTime)` and is shared **process-wide**, "In production, a service has exactly one container and therefore exactly one cached model" — and confirming `TenantedDbContext` registers NO analogous cache-key factory for `ITenantProvider` (only encryption gets that treatment) — surfaced a CONFIRMED, PREVIOUSLY-UNDETECTED DEFECT INDEPENDENT OF POOLING: a long-running multi-tenant service without `.WithEncryption()` enabled would have its tenant filter permanently frozen to whichever `ITenantProvider` instance constructed the very first `TenantedDbContext` in the process. The fix (same idiom EF Core's own docs recommend for exactly this multi-tenancy shape — binding the filter through `this`, which EF Core's query-filter compilation rebinds per EXECUTING instance rather than the model-building instance) closes both hazards at once: `SharedKernelDbContext` gains `CurrentUserContext`/`RefreshUserContext(IUserContext)`; interceptors read user context live off `eventData.Context` instead of their own captured field; `TenantedDbContext` gains `RefreshRequestContext(IUserContext, ITenantProvider)` and a filter rebuilt to read the CURRENT instance's `TenantProvider` at query time. New opt-in `EfCorePersistenceBuilder.WithDbContextPooling(int poolSize = 1024)` layers `AddPooledDbContextFactory<TContext>` plus a scoped `TContext` factory delegate that creates-then-refreshes, so existing direct-injection consumer code is unaffected; two `.Build()`-time guards block combining pooling with `.WithDbContextFactory()` (conflicting factory registrations) or `.WithEncryption()` (the identical `IEncryptionVersionOverride` staleness hazard is explicitly out of scope for this phase, recorded as a follow-up candidate, not silently left broken). **P-323**: `EncryptionOptionsKeyProvider` gains an internal singleton `EncryptionKeyByteCache` (`ConcurrentDictionary<string,byte[]>`, cleared via `IOptionsMonitor<EncryptionOptions>.OnChange`) so the common no-rotation-in-flight case decodes each key once, not per call — deliberately a SINGLETON, not scoped like the provider itself, since decoded key bytes vary only with config value, not per request; `GetByIdsAsync`'s "1000 IDs" doc guidance (an SQL-Server-`IN(...)`-expansion assumption) is corrected to describe Npgsql's actual `= ANY(@array)` single-parameter translation, with a re-derived ~50,000-ID memory-guardrail recommendation, alongside a new opt-in `IReadRepository.GetByIdsChunkedAsync` sibling that changes nothing about `GetByIdsAsync`'s own existing single-query behavior. **P-324**: all four packages gain a real `README.md` wired via `PackageReadmeFile` (previously wired in none, despite `08.Storage`/`09.Search`/`10.Intelligence`/`17.Workflows` already establishing the pattern); `06.Persistence/CLAUDE.md`'s `SharedKernelDbContext` constructor doc — verified against the real source in this same session — corrected from a fictitious 2-parameter shape to the actual 9 (`options`, three platform interceptors, `additionalInterceptors`, four encryption-related parameters); the `EfCorePersistenceExtensions.cs`/`EncryptedEntityBatchProcessorRegistry.cs` "reflection-free" wording inconsistency reconciled to the registry's own more accurate "justified, model-build-time exception" framing. **P-325**: `DbConnectionFactoryDiagnosticsExtensions.CheckReadinessAsync`'s `ExecuteScalar()` and `MigrationAndSeedHostedService`'s advisory-lock acquire/release `ExecuteNonQuery()` calls — confirmed via source read to be genuinely synchronous inside `async` methods — are corrected via a safe runtime cast to `System.Data.Common.DbCommand` and its true async members, with zero change to `IDbConnectionFactory`'s public interface; the release-path call MUST use `CancellationToken.None` unconditionally (not the caller's token) to preserve the existing synchronous version's always-completes unlock guarantee — using the original token would risk skipping the unlock under cancellation, a correctness regression this fix must not introduce. Overall Progress: Design 84 total/20○, Core 128/31○, Tests 97/37○, Docs 52/14○, Published 8/4○ (persistence-arch-planner, WO-051 batch 2 — work order complete)
- [2026-07-30] WO-051 batch 1 (P-315..P-320) planned — six focused phases added: D-65..D-76 (Design: 12 tasks), C-98..C-117 (Core: 20 tasks), T-61..T-82 (Tests: 22 tasks), DO-39..DO-46 (Docs: 8 tasks); 62 new tasks total; no new Scaffold or Published tasks (no new packages, no new NuGet dependencies — `System.Buffers.Binary`/`System.Diagnostics.ActivitySource` are BCL, `EnableRetryOnFailure` is already part of the already-referenced `Npgsql.EntityFrameworkCore.PostgreSQL`). This is batch 1 of 2 — a second batch (P-321..P-325) follows in a separate dispatch. Key decisions — **P-315**: confirmed and corrected a real, previously-undetected defect — `EntityTypeConfigurationBase`'s unconditional `.IsRowVersion()` on a plain PostgreSQL `bytea` column is provably non-functional (nothing in Postgres auto-populates an arbitrary `bytea` on UPDATE), and the `ConcurrencyTokenConvention` its own XML doc (and D-05) had claimed existed was never built because a provider-branching convention inside `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` is architecturally impossible (`.UseXminAsConcurrencyToken()` is Npgsql-only; EfCore must never reference Npgsql). Corrected split: EfCore marks `.IsConcurrencyToken()` only (provider-neutral); a new `SharedKernel.Persistence.PostgreSQL` `XminConcurrencyTokenConvention`/`XminRowVersionValueConverter` binds that property to the real `xmin` system column via a `byte[]`↔`uint` converter. D-05 corrected in place with an explicit supersession note. **P-316**: `TenantedRepository`'s two `GetByIdForTenantAsync*` methods migrate their `EF.Property<TId>(e,"Id")` primary-key predicate to the same expression-tree pattern `EfRepository.ExistsAsync` already uses (P-099) — a sibling class that never received the P-105-era fix its own changelog implied was complete. **P-317** (depends on 03.Domain's `KeysetSpecification<T,TKey>`/P-308, not yet implemented there — new Cross-Domain Dependencies row added): new `ISpecificationEvaluator<T>.GetKeysetQuery<TKey>` (a method-level generic parameter beyond `T`, avoiding any TKey-erasure reflection) translates `AfterKey`/`AfterId` into a genuine seek predicate; new `IReadRepository.ListKeysetAsync<TKey>` + `KeysetPage<TAggregate,TKey>` give callers `HasMore`/next-cursor via a Take+1 probe row — a second explicitly documented deviation from "Skip/Take always last" (after `StreamAsync`'s forced `AsNoTracking`), since Skip is never applied for a keyset spec. Offset paging (`ListPagedAsync`/`PagedSpecification<T>`) is completely unchanged. **P-318** (also depends on P-308): `AsSplitQuery` applied at new pipeline step 2c (after StringIncludes, before OrderBy) in `GetQuery`/`GetProjectedQuery`/`GetKeysetQuery<TKey>`; default `false` behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged. **P-319**: automatic `.TagWith(spec.GetType().Name)` on every generated query, zero configuration; new `SharedKernel.Persistence`/`"1.0"` `ActivitySource` (naming mirrors `02.Caching`'s `SharedKernel.Caching`/`"1.0"` precedent exactly) plus domain-local `PersistenceTagKeys` wrap `EfRepository`/`EfReadRepository` operations in spans tagged with aggregate type/operation/outcome — reusing `01.Core`'s existing `WellKnownTagKeys.ErrorType` for the failure case rather than inventing a duplicate key; never a raw parameter/entity/tenant value in any tag. **This ActivitySource name/version is the coordination point for `13.ServiceDefaults`' paired phase P-326** (per the WO-051 dispatcher note) and is recorded in `06.Persistence/CLAUDE.md`'s new Observability section. **P-320**: `UsePostgreSQL(...)` gains optional `EnableRetryOnFailure` parameters (the only legal Npgsql call site — same layering constraint P-315 resolved); `EfCorePersistenceBuilder.WithTransientFaultRetry()` is a documented convenience/DI-registration pairing partner, not itself capable of configuring Npgsql. The genuine correction: EF Core's retrying execution strategy requires the whole transactional unit inside one `ExecuteAsync` delegate, which the existing handle-based `BeginTransactionAsync()`/`CommitAsync()` API structurally cannot satisfy — new `ITransactionalUnitOfWork.ExecuteInTransactionAsync`/`<TResult>` is the retry-safe alternative (reusing the existing begin/commit machinery and P-105's deferred-dispatch-until-commit behavior), and `EfTransactionalUnitOfWork.BeginTransactionAsync` gains an unconditional `RetriesOnFailure` guard (independent of whether `.WithTransientFaultRetry()` was called) that fails fast with an actionable, platform-specific message instead of EF Core's native one. Overall Progress: Design 76 total/12○, Core 117/20○, Tests 82/22○, Docs 46/8○ (persistence-arch-planner, WO-051 batch 1)

Key decisions — **P-333** (structured-logging retrofit + tracing gap): `01.Core`'s `LoggingEventIdRanges` registry already reserves `6000-6999` for this domain but nothing in it has ever emitted a `[LoggerMessage]` log — every described logging addition (`ConcurrencyInterceptor` conflict detection, `MigrationAndSeedHostedService` lifecycle + advisory lock, transient-fault retry attempt/exhaustion, `EncryptionRotationService` batch progress) lands in `SharedKernel.Persistence.EfCore` exclusively, since `SharedKernel.Persistence.Abstractions` is a pure interface library with no `ILogger` consumer at all — resolved as: Abstractions claims no sub-block, EfCore (the first package that actually needs one) claims `6000-6099`. Retry-attempt logging is designed as an EF-Core-core-only `DiagnosticListener` subscription against `CoreEventId.ExecutionStrategyRetrying` (a provider-neutral core-assembly event, not Npgsql's), preserving the "EfCore never references Npgsql" hard rule; exhaustion logging wraps the platform's own two save boundaries (`EfUnitOfWork`/`EfTransactionalUnitOfWork`) rather than needing a third hook. Closes the `EfRepository.GetByIdAsync`/`.ExistsAsync` tracing gap into the existing `RepositoryTracing` helper. **P-334** (real config-section binding): confirmed a genuine doc/code mismatch — `EncryptionOptions`/`PersistenceServiceOptions` each claim a bound configuration section in their own XML doc, but neither `.WithEncryption()` nor `.WithServiceName()` ever calls `IConfiguration.GetSection(...)`. Adds `SectionName` constants plus new `IConfiguration`-accepting overloads on both builder methods (via `Microsoft.Extensions.Options.ConfigurationExtensions`'s `.Bind(IConfiguration)`), composing with the pre-existing `Action<T>`/direct-value paths under normal `IOptions<T>` later-registration-wins semantics — the existing code paths are completely unchanged. **P-336** (Testcontainers fixture consolidation): verified the drift is WORSE than the phase input described — not "one drifted copy" in `PostgreSQL.Tests`, but FOUR independent test classes each spinning up their own inline `PostgreSqlContainer` (all pinned to the obsolete `postgres:16-alpine`/`new XBuilder().WithImage(...)` shape `16.Testing` already moved off of at its own 4.13.0 bump) plus a fifth, genuinely dead, never-instantiated `Fixtures/PostgreSqlContainerFixture.cs`, plus a sixth ad hoc copy inlined directly in `Dapper.Tests`' one Postgres-touching test class — six drifted copies total, not two. All four `PostgreSQL.Tests` classes already carry `[Collection("PostgreSQL")]` with no matching `[CollectionDefinition]` anywhere in the assembly (confirmed via repo-wide grep) — clearly intended sharing that was never wired up; the consolidation plan completes it via one new `[CollectionDefinition("PostgreSQL")] : ICollectionFixture<PostgreSqlContainerFixture>` declaration rather than leaving each class to independently adopt the shared fixture. Both `.Tests.csproj` files already reference `SharedKernel.Testing` — no new project reference needed. **P-337** (restore + command timeout bundle): single-entity restore is designed as a new `IRestorableRepository<TAggregate,TId>` in Abstractions (ORM-agnostic signature, unlike `IBulkMutationRepository`) implemented unconditionally by `EfRepository<TAggregate,TId>` with a runtime `ISoftDeletable` guard — mirroring the already-shipped `BulkSpecificationGuard` precedent rather than inventing a parallel `TenantedRepository`-style base-class hierarchy; restore stages via `ChangeTracker.CurrentValues` exactly like the interceptors already do, so `AuditInterceptor` sets `ModifiedBy`/`ModifiedOn` automatically with zero interceptor changes. Bulk restore needs NO new production code at all — it is a documented USAGE PATTERN of the already-shipped `ExecuteUpdateAsync`, closed purely by a Docs task. Command timeout is placed on `EfCorePersistenceBuilder` (EfCore), not `UsePostgreSQL(...)` (PostgreSQL package) — a deliberate correction of the phase input's own framed ambiguity — because `DbContextOptionsBuilder.CommandTimeout(int?)` is a provider-NEUTRAL `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Relational` extension already reachable from EfCore, unlike `EnableRetryOnFailure`, which genuinely is Npgsql-only. **P-338** (read-replica routing): the hardest design in this batch — routes `IReadRepository` operations to a second, lazily-constructed `TContext` instance built via `ActivatorUtilities.CreateInstance<TContext>` against a keyed replica `DbContextOptions<TContext>`, deliberately reusing the SAME scope-ambient, DI-resolved interceptor instances (and their live `CurrentUserContext` per WO-051/P-322) the primary context already resolved, so audit consistency is automatic. `EfReadRepository` gains one new OPTIONAL nullable constructor parameter (`IReadReplicaContextAccessor<TContext>? = null`) resolved fresh on every call (never cached) since transaction state can change between calls on the same injected repository; the write side (`IRepository`/`EfRepository`) is completely untouched. The "never route inside an active transaction" hard requirement is enforced by checking live `Database.CurrentTransaction` state, the same class of unconditional live-state check `EfTransactionalUnitOfWork`'s existing retry guard already uses. **P-339** (pgvector nearest-neighbor ergonomics): confirmed `HasVectorColumn`/`VectorColumnAttribute` today do nothing beyond raw `HasColumnType("vector(N)")` — zero query-side help exists. Resolved the interface-access problem (a new helper living in the PostgreSQL package cannot call `Specification<T>`'s `protected AddOrderBy` from outside its own subclass hierarchy) by having `VectorOrderingExpressions.ByDistance<TAggregate>(...)` return the boxed `Expression<Func<TAggregate,object>>` directly rather than trying to call `AddOrderBy` itself — the CONSUMER's own `Specification<TAggregate>` subclass wires it into its own protected `AddOrderBy` call from its own constructor, exactly how `KeysetSpecification<T,TKey>` already populates protected members. The `MethodInfo` for `VectorExtensions.CosineDistance`/`.L2Distance` is captured via a statically-typed delegate-cast `.Method` property — resolved by the C# compiler, never `Type.GetMethod`/`MakeGenericMethod` — satisfying the platform's reflection-elimination rule with zero exception needed. Explicitly scoped to `Vector`-typed properties only (`float[]`-typed vector columns are out of scope for this helper, a documented limitation). No Layering Rules, Package Naming, or new-package changes anywhere in this dispatch — every phase extends an already-shipped package's surface (persistence-arch-planner, user request, WO-053, P-333/P-334/P-336/P-337/P-338/P-339)
